# Vision Fidelity Ledger — v6 (2026-10-04) — MILESTONE M1 re-run

**Supersedes v5 as the current assessment.** v5 is kept whole at `VISION_FIDELITY_LEDGER_v5.md` (its
Tier-1 entries are the ones P1.17 closed and the register rows cite), as v3 is at `VISION_FIDELITY_LEDGER_v3.md`.
This edition re-measures the product after P1.17, the second truth pass, as the M1 line requires — the Tier-1
count is measured again, never declared: a fresh six-region assessment against HEAD `e0d13ab1` (port :8086,
single-user mode, `AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1`). Under that flag the gateway routes every model call to the
deterministic native floor — the configuration CI runs and the one any machine without a local model gets
(it is NOT the shipped default: with the flag unset and Ollama discoverable, the gateway serves from the
local model). What IS assessable on the floor is whether every floor-served surface discloses it.

**Tiers.** Each finding carries the tier the refuter stands behind — 1: a TRUTH DEFECT (a reached surface
tells the user something untrue, fabricates a figure, or certifies what it could not assess); 2: an
INVISIBLE SHORTFALL (partial, and the surface does not say so); 3: a CAPABILITY GAP that is disclosed or
not reached. DELIVERED entries carry no tier.

## How this document was generated — and what that means for reading it

Six assessors ran one vision region each against the booted HEAD, explicitly barred from three
sources: the vision's own §16, the previous edition of this ledger, and `AUTONOMOUS_PROGRESS.md`
(a record of intent, not proof). They executed routes, read handlers and components, and counted
stores. The ASSESSMENT was capped at ten findings per region, most consequential first — and every
region returned exactly ten, so **60 is the size of the cap, not the size of the gap**; a region's
eleventh-worst thing is not in this ledger. **Every finding was then attacked by an independent
refuter instructed to default to refuted** (v2 refuted six per region), who had to reproduce the gap (execute the
route, read the code, count the store) before letting it stand, and who was told to correct the
verdict UP or DOWN when the assessor had it wrong.

Reading rules that follow from the method:

1. **DELIVERED is understated by construction.** Assessors were told their job was the gap that
   remains, but to report DELIVERED where they verified it. Read the DELIVERED entries as the
   floor of what works, not the ceiling.
2. **Every one of the 60 findings was individually refuted.** The refuters reproduced
   48 as stated and overturned 12 — 0 moved to a
   HARSHER verdict (a DELIVERED claim that was not), 9 to a MILDER one (a STUB that was real
   machinery with an undisclosed shortfall). The verdict in each heading is the one the REFUTER
   stands behind; the assessor's original is shown where it differs.
2a. **And on the TIER, which is the axis M1 is scored on:** 4 finding(s) were made HARSHER by the refuter and 12 milder, with **4 escalated INTO tier 1** — the M1 measure itself. A refuter can tighten a tier while leaving the verdict untouched, and reporting only the verdict hid that: 2 of the escalations into tier 1 had the verdict axis call them MILDER.
3. **The floor is the environment.** A finding that says 'floor scaffold reached the user' is
   not a complaint that no model ran — it is a finding that the surface did not SAY so, or
   certified what it could not assess. That is the §15 principle-6 line, and it binds.
4. **Refuters reproduced with their OWN inputs.** Several verdicts below carry evidence from a
   second journey, VSB, change-record or transfer the refuter created on the live backend —
   a finding that reproduces under a second, independently chosen input is stronger evidence
   than one observation.

## R1 — §10 + §11 — the solution-quality bar, continuous compliance, and the faith-content constitution

### R1.0 · The Constitution page prints "Verified by open — … This article is currently UNMET" as a verification — **PARTIAL** *(assessed DOC_OVERCLAIM)* · tier 2 *(assessed tier 1)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A governance surface a reader goes to in order to check the platform's constitutional compliance states that an article is verified, using as its evidence a sentence that says the opposite. It is a truth defect on a reached surface about the platform's own honesty rules — the most self-undermining class there is.
- **claim:** §11 — "Compliance engines (Halal/Sharia · UK-Legal/London · Regulatory · EHS · Ethical · Constitutional/gaas) are integrated into every synthesis/generative/operational workflow, not bolted on."
- **observed:** Executed GET /api/v1/religion-free route http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v154/constitution/articles → 33 articles. Article 8 returns {"title":"A COUNT IS COMPUTED, NOT ASSERTED", "verified":"open — `api/method.py:719` states \"eleven of seventy were defended\" as prose beside a computed", "unmet": false, "unmet_reason": null}. The handler at agentic_core/api/integration_surface.py:190-191 captures only the FIRST line of the canon's multi-line `*Verified:*` block; the canon's own next lines (agentic_core/constitution/CONSTITUTION_canonical.md:84-89) continue "…`f\"{len(wanted)} of {len(classes)}\"` nine lines above (FU-305). This article is currently **UNMET** and names its own breach." The `unmet` flag is driven only by `^Status:\s*UNMET` (integration_surface.py:196), and this article's Status line says "MET as of W515", so unmet=false. apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/governance/ConstitutionalUI.tsx:279-283 then renders `art.verified` under the literal label "Verified by ", and the page's own summary block (lines 251-264) names only Art. 11, 13, 21 as recording a breach.
- **evidence:** Page text as rendered: "Verified by open — `api/method.py:719` states \"eleven of seventy were defended\" as prose beside a computed" — the word "Verified" prefixing a sentence whose own next clause says the article is UNMET.
- **refutation: STOOD, verdict CORRECTED DOC_OVERCLAIM → PARTIAL.** The OBSERVATION reproduces exactly: the handler captures only the first line of the canon's multi-line `*Verified:*` block, and the Constitution page prints the resulting half-sentence "Verified by open — `api/method.py:719` states \"eleven of seventy were defended\" as prose beside a computed" with no Unmet row. That is a real surface defect and it stands.  But the assessor's TIER-1 PREMISE is refuted. They argued the page certifies as verified an article that is actually UNMET, hiding the canon's own "This article is currently **UNMET**" continuation. The canon's authoritative `*Status:*` line at CONSTITUTION_canonical.md:89 says "MET as of W515 — the figure is now read live from the ledger, and the answer refuses rather than substituting when the ledger cannot be parsed". The assessor quoted that line in their own evidence and then reasoned past it. I verified the Status line is TRUE, not a counter-claim: the hardcoded literal is gone, `_defended_share()` reads docs/FABRICATION_LEDGER.md at call time, the refusal branch exists rather than a substitution, and the live route I drove with my own input returned the figure computed from the ledger. So `unmet: false` is CORRECT. The stale artifact is the canon's pre-W515 `*Verified:*` prose, which was never updated when the Status line was appended.  That inverts the sign of the finding. The page is not over-claiming compliance — it is printing a RESOLVED breach as an open one, making the platform look worse than it is. Nothing is fabricated (the figure it quotes is the old prose, now superseded), nothing is certified that could not be assessed (`verified_basis` honestly reports "named in the article", i.e. the document's own claim rather than a run check), and the article's actual verdict is right. "DOC_OVERCLAIM" is the wrong class, and a surface understating its own compliance is not the self-undermining tier-1 defect described. Per the brief's rule — a tier-1 claim must show a reached surface telling a user something untrue in the flattering direction, fabricating a figure, or certifying what it could not assess — this is none of those.  Two genuine shortfalls remain, which is why this is PARTIAL rather than REFUTED, at tier 2 rather than 3 (a reader does come away believing the count breach is still open, so it is not cosmetic): (a) integration_surface.py:183 captures one line, garbling any multi-line Verified block — I measured the blast radius as 3 of 33 articles, with only Art. 8 left as a bare self-contradicting fragment; (b) line 198's regex matches only `Status: UNMET`, so there is no "met"/"status" field on the response at all — the very line that supersedes the stale prose cannot reach the page, leaving the reader no way to see the resolution. Real machinery (33 articles parsed, breach list, no-mechanism list, honest basis strings, both branches carrying the summary fields) with an undisclosed shortfall: the MILDER case.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed GET http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v154/constitution/articles myself: 33 articles, Art. 8 = {"verified":"open — `api/method.py:719` states \"eleven of seventy were defended\" as prose beside a computed", "unmet":false, "unmet_reason":null}. Read agentic_core/api/integration_surface.py:183-202 (the single-line `^\*?Verified:\*?\s*(.+?)$` capture and the `^\*?Status:\s*UNMET\b` flag) and apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/governance/ConstitutionalUI.tsx:279-283 (renders `art.verified` under the literal label "Verified by "). Then went beyond the assessor with my own inputs: (1) swept ALL 33 articles, re-parsing each full multi-line `*Verified:*` block from agentic_core/constitution/CONSTITUTION_canonical.md and diffing it against the API's capture — exactly three truncate with a flagged continuation (8, 11, 13), and 11/13 both carry unmet=true so the page renders their "Unmet —" row, leaving Art. 8 the only contradictory card; (2) tested the premise that Art. 8 is actually unmet by reading agentic_core/api/method.py — the literal "eleven of seventy" is GONE repo-wide (grep, excluding _archive) and `_defended_share()` at ~line 752 parses docs/FABRICATION_LEDGER.md at call time with an explicit NOT-ASSESSABLE refusal branch instead of a fallback; (3) executed POST /api/v1/method/screen with my OWN path list ["agentic_core/api/method.py"], which returned "11 of 70 proposals were defended ... read live from docs/FABRICATION_LEDGER.md" — the count is computed, not asserted.

### R1.1 · The halal tool promises ingredient flags; the only real screen it computes, and the three sections the floor withholds, never reach the screen — **API_ONLY** · tier 1

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The page promises three things by name, the route withholds all three and says so, and the sentence saying so is the one thing the page does not render. The reader is left with a long document under headings like "## Recommended Certifying Bodies" that contains no certifying bodies, and no word that anything was withheld — on faith content, where §11 says the honesty constraints bind hardest.
- **claim:** §11 rule 4 — "Floor-served output is never presented as scholarship or translation … and every AI-served surface renders serving provenance"; Owner ruling 2026-09-29 — halal is assessed by certificate verification, the body clears, never the platform.
- **observed:** Executed POST /api/v1/religion/halal-review {"product_name":"Gummy Bears","product_description":"a pork gelatin gummy sweet","ingredients":["pork gelatin","glucose syrup"]}. The response carries the substantive work in `ingredient_screen` (pork gelatin flagged, glucose syrup unmatched, verdict null) plus `sections_withheld: ["Halal Status Assessment","Critical Issues","Flagged Ingredients"]` and a `floor_note` explaining why. The surface is apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/ReligionHub.tsx:197-210: a DomainTool with resultKey="assessment" and NO `renderExtra` — the only one of the four religion tools without one (fatwa 142, tafsir 172, hadith 225 all have one). DomainTool renders only `displayText` + `result.disclaimer` (components/DomainTool.tsx:316-321); `floor_note` and `sections_withheld` appear only inside `withDisclosures` (line 79-83), which feeds save/copy/download, not the screen.
- **evidence:** ReligionHub.tsx:199 — "Workstation's own AI gives a halal pre-assessment (ingredient flags, process concerns, certification guidance)" — against floor_note: "served by the deterministic native floor — no halal status, critical issues or flagged ingredients are offered."
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced on every leg with independently chosen input, and the tier-1 case is in fact stronger than the assessor argued. API_ONLY is the right verdict: ingredient_screen is genuine, substantive machinery (deterministic 34-term screen, per-ingredient reasons, verdict deliberately null, an honest basis string) that reaches the API and no screen anywhere in the frontend. Tier 1 holds, and I deliberately did NOT rest it on "a model did not run" — that is AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1, the environment — nor on missing serving provenance, because the screen does carry "[Workstation native structured engine - owned, no external dependency]", so that leg of §11 rule 4 is met. The tier rests on three environment-independent facts. First, the page promises "ingredient flags" by name at ReligionHub.tsx:199 and the screen renders zero flags. Second, three sections are withheld and the word "withheld" never appears on the reached surface: the reader gets a complete-looking document under "## Recommended Certifying Bodies" that names no certifying body and instead echoes back the submitted prompt. Third and most damning, my Zamzam probe shows the floor ASSERTS fabricated cross-contamination exposures for sealed single-ingredient bottled water, built from request bigrams, in a section whose own preamble says "surface, don't invent". That is a reached surface stating something untrue about faith content, and it is the floor's own composition defect, not the environment's — exactly the class W498 already fixed for the hadith tool by withholding bigram-filled sections. Halal withholds three sections but leaves Cross-Contamination Risks filled and renders neither the floor_note nor the ingredient screen. Against the Owner ruling 2026-09-29 (the certifying body clears, never the platform) and §11 binding hardest on faith content, this is tier 1. The one counterpoint, which does not change the tier: users who download/copy/save DO get floor_note via withDisclosures, but the screen everyone reaches first carries none of it. Direction SAME because verdict label and tier are both unchanged; my added fabrication leg strengthens the evidence without moving either.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed POST /api/v1/religion/halal-review twice with MY OWN inputs, not the assessor's gummy bears. (1) Marshmallow Fluff Spread {bovine gelatin, vanilla extract, corn syrup, carmine}, process "whipped and pasteurised", markets UK/Indonesia: response carries a real ingredient_screen (3 flagged with reasons, unmatched ["corn syrup"], verdict null, honest basis string) plus sections_withheld ["Halal Status Assessment","Critical Issues","Flagged Ingredients"] and a floor_note. (2) A harder probe of my own design: Zamzam Bottled Water, single ingredient "water", "sealed at source" — the rendered assessment still asserts four cross-contamination exposures ("Exposure on zamzam bottled", "Exposure on plain bottled", "Exposure on bottled drinking", "Exposure on drinking water"), and a programmatic check on that same response returned `screen says withheld? False` with the floor_note text absent from the assessment. Read the code myself: ReligionHub.tsx:196-210 halal DomainTool has resultKey="assessment" and NO renderExtra, against renderExtra present at lines 142/172/225 for dialogue/tafsir/hadith; Grep for "ingredient_screen" across all of apps/workstation-superapp/src returns NO MATCHES; DomainTool.tsx:159 displayText = refinedText ?? resultText rendered at :319, while floor_note/sections_withheld enter only withDisclosures (:79-83) feeding exportText (:163) = save/copy/download.

### R1.2 · The Compliance page names four consumers of the §11 screen; two of them never call it — **DOC_OVERCLAIM** · tier 1

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A compliance surface asserting coverage it does not have is the exact inversion of its own stated discipline ("It flags; it does not certify"). A reader is told the riba/alcohol objective they just ran through the Forge passed a Sharia/Halal screen; it did not reach one.
- **claim:** §11 — compliance engines "are integrated into every synthesis/generative/operational workflow, not bolted on"
- **observed:** apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/governance/ComplianceChecker.tsx:48 states the screen is "Used by the economy, synthesis, Genesis and the Forge." Grepped every importer of the module: genesis.py:70 ✓, economy/charity.py:227 ✓, swarm.py:287 ✓, marketplace.py:325 ✓, heartbeat.py:75 ✓, v138/ceo.py:322 ✓, adaptive_profiles.py:72 ✓, vbs/quality.py:334 ✓, resource_fabric.py:809 ✓. But `grep -n "screen_compliance|assure_delivery" agentic_core/api/forge.py` → no match (275 lines; the word "quality" occurs once, inside a prompt string at line 178); same grep on agentic_core/synthesis/api.py → no match; same on agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py → no match. Executed POST /api/v1/forge/run with objective "a riba-based interest lending product funded by alcohol and gambling revenue" → HTTP 200, a full "## Path to Commercialisation", and governance:{"status":"allowed","content_screened":false}.
- **evidence:** Forge response: "scope":"intent + domain only — this gate screened the request's intent label against the prohibited list … The delivery's content was NOT screened" — while the compliance page tells the reader the federated screen is "Used by … the Forge."
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding reproduces exactly as assessed, on a second independently chosen objective, and I confirmed the one thing that could have refuted it. Both named consumers that the assessor says never call the screen genuinely do not: forge.py reaches no compliance leg even transitively (it calls gateway.query_meta directly rather than the gating ai_text() wrapper in _ai_provenance.py, which was the plausible escape route a name-only grep would have missed), and the Synthesis Studio path (/synthesis -> /api/v1/synthesis/generate, and /api/v1/studio/synthesise) has no compliance call either.  Tier 1 holds, and the three tier-1 escape hatches do not apply. This is not the environment: AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1 changes who WROTE the text, not whether a deterministic regex/engine screen ran, and screen_compliance is deterministic — I ran it live and it returned a real "fail" in the same session. Nor does the surface disclose it. The Forge's own JSON is honest about ITSELF ("content_screened": false), but that disclosure sits on a different surface and does not reach the reader of the Compliance page, who is told the opposite; the synthesis path carries no disclosure whatever. And the untruth is material rather than nominal: the exact text my Forge run returned HTTP 200 for would have come back sharia_halal FAIL ("haram term present: 'pork'") had it reached the screen the page says it reaches. A user who reads "It flags; it does not certify" and then "Used by ... the Forge" has been told their Forge output was flagged by a Sharia/Halal screen that never read it — a compliance surface overstating its own coverage is the inversion of the discipline it states on the same line.  Two honest qualifications that narrow the finding without lowering the tier. First, "synthesis" is the weaker of the two: there IS an internal thing called synthesis that is gated — swarm.py:207 runs assure_delivery (hence screen_compliance) on the ceo_synthesis output under label "delegate", and swarm.py:752 does the same under label "cascade". So for that word the sentence is ambiguous rather than flatly false. But the user-facing referent is unambiguous — SearchMeshModal.tsx:29 labels route /synthesis "Synthesis Studio" — and that surface is ungated and, unlike the Forge, says nothing about it. Second, "the Forge" admits no such ambiguity: there is exactly one Forge (SearchMeshModal.tsx:46, App.tsx:205 ForgePipeline -> /api/v1/forge/*), and it does not call the screen. The assessor's count of two is therefore right on the strict reading and right on the user's reading; only the mechanism behind "synthesis" is more nuanced than their grep showed.  Scope note on my own work: I executed routes and read files only. I wrote no repository file; the two response bodies I captured went to the session scratchpad. My Forge run did append one row to the forge_runs store (named REFUTER-OWN-INPUT) and the synthesis run appended one output under the synthesis output dir — unavoidable side effects of executing the routes as instructed, flagged here so they are not mistaken for pre-existing data.
- **refuter's evidence:** 1) Read the surface myself: C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\apps\workstation-superapp\src\pages\governance\ComplianceChecker.tsx:48 — "Used by the economy, synthesis, Genesis and the Forge." — inside the UNCONDITIONAL <header> paragraph (no {cond && ...} gate). Reachability confirmed by code, not by name: App.tsx:231 redirects /compliance -> /governance-hub?tab=compliance, and GovernanceCenter.tsx:6,14 registers tab id 'compliance' with El: ComplianceChecker. 2) Grepped the CALL, repo-wide over agentic_core (Grep tool; the bash grep was stalling on node_modules): screen_compliance/assure_delivery call sites are compliance.py, vbs/quality.py, governance/adaptive_profiles.py, economy/charity.py:227, api/genesis.py:70, api/marketplace.py:325, organism/heartbeat.py:75, api/swarm.py:287, api/v138/ceo.py:322, plus the assure_delivery consumers (deliverables, products, vsb, resource_fabric, management_systems, _ai_provenance). agentic_core/api/forge.py, agentic_core/synthesis/api.py and agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py appear in NONE of them. 3) Checked the transitive route the assessor did not (my memory note: grep the call, not the method name) — agentic_core/api/_ai_provenance.py:60 DOES call assure_delivery, so a caller going through ai_text() would be gated. forge.py:145 calls gateway.query_meta directly and never imports _ai_provenance, so it does not reach that leg. 4) MY OWN INPUT through the Forge: POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/forge/run objective "A pork-charcuterie and craft-beer subscription box financed by a payday loan at 1200% APR, marketed to gambling addicts", stages [generator] -> HTTP 200, full deliverable returned, response keys contain NO compliance/quality/screen key at all, governance = {"status":"allowed","screened":"intent + domain","content_screened":false,"scope":"...The delivery's content was NOT screened...","checkpoint":"CHK-1791090008502","node":"forge-node"}. 5) MY OWN INPUT through synthesis: traced /synthesis -> SynthesisStudio.tsx:237 -> /api/v1/synthesis/generate; POST with instructions "Write a launch plan for a riba-based payday lending product funded by alcohol and gambling revenue" -> HTTP 200, 3051-byte report with Abstract/Introduction/Analysis/Discussion/Conclusion; response keys are output_id, output_url, content, metadata, timestamp, served_by, is_external — no compliance field and no disclosure of any kind. 6) Materiality: POST /api/v1/compliance/check with the SAME objective I sent the Forge -> overall "fail", compliant false, sharia_halal "fail — haram term present in the text: 'pork' ... Engine also flags: HARAM_ELEM".

### R1.3 · All 33 constitutional articles are served with an empty body, and 22 of 33 verification lines are cut off mid-sentence — **STUB** · tier 2

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The page is the platform's constitution. Every card shows a title, an empty body where the rule should be, and a sentence fragment labelled "Verified by". Nothing on the page says the body was not loaded, so the reader cannot tell a rule with no text from a rule with no content.
- **claim:** §11 — the Constitutional/gaas engine as a live compliance engine; Article 33's own contract that the document names its unmet articles
- **observed:** agentic_core/api/integration_surface.py:188 hardcodes `"content": ""` for every parsed article; the `verified` field (line 191) is `re.search(r"(?im)^\*?Verified:\*?\s*(.+?)\s*$", _body)` — a single-line capture. Measured the live response: 33 of 33 articles have content == "", and 22 of 33 `verified` strings end mid-phrase (Art. 1 "…each with a basis) across", Art. 3 "…`assure_delivery` returns", Art. 9 "…provenance headers on exports", Art. 11 "…`economy/revenue.py` surfaces", Art. 15 "…the Owner rulings"). ConstitutionalUI.tsx:274 renders `{art.content}` as the article text.
- **evidence:** Live: {"id":"1","title":"A PLATFORM MAY ONLY REPORT WHAT IT DID","content":"","verified":"the three-state verdict discipline (`MET` / `UNMET` / `NOT_ASSESSABLE`, each with a basis) across"} — and the canon has the rule text at CONSTITUTION_canonical.md right below each heading.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding reproduces exactly in class and slightly worse in magnitude. `content` is a hardcoded empty string for all 33 articles even though the parser is holding the body text, and the page prints that empty string as the article text; 26 `verified` lines are abridged to their first physical line, 25 of them mid-sentence. Two corrections to the assessor's premise, both of which leave the verdict intact: the truncation count is 26 (25 visibly mid-sentence), not 22; and the canon does NOT have rule text under every heading — 6 articles genuinely have none. That second correction actually sharpens the harm they named: the page now renders the same blank paragraph for an article the document never gave a body (Art. 20) and for one whose two-sentence rule was loaded and thrown away (Art. 1), with nothing saying which is which. STUB is right: the field the surface renders has no mechanism behind it at all, and no guard asserts it. I hold the tier at 2 rather than raising it, on the M1 test. The blank body is an omission, not an assertion — no figure is fabricated and no sentence on the card states an outcome the platform did not produce. The abridged "Verified by …" lines are visibly severed fragments ("…across", "…records"), so they under-claim rather than certify. Most importantly, the document's own breach disclosures survive the truncation on a separate channel: Art. 11, 13 and 21 are flagged `unmet` from the `Status:` field and the page prints an explicit "Unmet — <reason>" line for each (live: articles_recording_a_breach = ["11","13","21"]), so the dropped halves of those Verified lines ("Thirteen callers remain…", "still do not say so…", "spec-only…") do not leave a shortfall hidden. The one case that comes closest to tier 1 is Art. 5, where the cut lands on a period so the reader gets no cue and the dropped remainder is the document's own limitation ("what the harness does NOT measure is whether a guard asserts the RIGHT property, and its coverage is the blind list and not the codebase") — a caveat stripped from a verification claim. But the rendered sentence is still the document's own words, not a new assertion, and it is one article of 33. Tier 2: a governance surface whose rule bodies are structurally absent and undisclosed, not a surface stating a falsehood.
- **refuter's evidence:** 1) Read C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\api\integration_surface.py:162-204 — the parser builds `_body` for every article and then hardcodes `"content": ""` (line 188); `verified` (line 191) is `re.search(r"(?im)^\*?Verified:\*?\s*(.+?)\s*$", _body)`, a single-LINE capture with a `[:400]` cap. 2) Executed the live route myself: `curl http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v154/constitution/articles` → 33 articles, `content == ""` for 33/33 (0 non-empty, 0 null), canon_present true, canon_basis "parsed 33 article heading(s) from the canonical markdown". 3) With MY OWN measurement rather than the assessor's spot-check, I re-parsed agentic_core/constitution/CONSTITUTION_canonical.md with the endpoint's own heading regex and compared every article to the served record: (a) 27 of 33 articles DO carry rule text between the heading and the `*Verified:*` line — 3,269 characters in total — and all of it is discarded; the remaining 6 (Art. 12, 19, 20, 26, 30, 32) have NO body in the document at all, only a title and a Verified line; (b) 26 of 33 `verified` strings are cut short of the document's full Verified block, and 25 of 33 end with no sentence terminator at all (Art. 1 "…each with a basis) across", Art. 2 "…and returns `None`", Art. 10 "…provenance from an unrecorded one;", Art. 28 "…`change_control_handoff` records", Art. 33 "…so the document cannot") — not 22. No string hit the 400-char cap (longest served is Art. 5 at 397), so every truncation is the single-line regex. 4) Confirmed the surface and its reachability: apps\workstation-superapp\src\pages\governance\ConstitutionalUI.tsx:274 renders `<p …>{art.content}</p>` (always empty) under the title; GovernanceCenter.tsx:13 mounts it as the Constitution tab and App.tsx:230 redirects /constitution to /governance-hub?tab=constitution. Read the whole page: the only disclosure blocks are `canon-absent` (gated on `canon_present === false`, so dead here), the breach/no-mechanism summary, and the per-article "names no verification mechanism" fallback — none of them mentions the body. 5) Checked the guard: integration_tests/test_mvp_spine.py:28371 asserts `verified` is non-empty and distinguishes None from "", but never touches `content`, so nothing covers the dropped body.

### R1.4 · The flagship 9-stage Concept-to-Commercialisation cascade runs no §11 screen and no §10 gate — including its own REGULATORY MAP stage — **PARTIAL** · tier 2

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Real machinery exists one module away and this workflow does not reach it. The stage prose labels itself a "deterministic scaffold", but nothing in the response or the spawned VSB entity records that the cascade was never compliance-screened or quality-gated, so the shortfall is invisible at the level that matters (the run).
- **claim:** §11 — "Compliance engines … are integrated into every synthesis/generative/operational workflow, not bolted on"; §10 — every solution is "compliant · verified · tested · validated"
- **observed:** Executed POST /api/v1/studio/synthesise {"challenge":"design a solar-powered water purifier for rural Kenya"} → 200, 9 stages in 3386 ms, stage 6 = "REGULATORY MAP", spawning entity vsb-3599bcca. `grep -n "screen_compliance|assure_delivery" agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py` → no match: the module's self-description (lines 1-24) calls itself "the flagship BTO workflow" with a "Regulatory / legal / compliance map", and no compliance screen or §10 quality record is computed anywhere in it. By contrast /api/v1/swarm/cascade, which I also executed with mission "launch a halal meal subscription funded by riba interest loans", returned quality.compliance.overall="fail" on 'riba' plus `bar_measured` over all 16 §10 criteria and `compliance_routed_to_cca` — so the machinery exists and this route does not use it.
- **evidence:** Stage 6 content: "## Health and Safety Requirements … ## Ethical Compliance Requirements … - Likelihood × impact to be scored; mitigations and owners to be assigned." — delivered under a REGULATORY MAP heading with no §11 verdict attached to the run.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding reproduces exactly as stated, on two independently chosen challenges, and the premise holds at the code level (no call site exists, so it is structural, not input-dependent). The verdict PARTIAL is right: the 9-stage cascade is real machinery that genuinely runs and persists a governed entity, but its §11 integration is absent and no §10 quality record is produced, including in its own REGULATORY MAP stage.  Tier 2 is right and I checked hard for tier 1 before saying so. Tier 1 needs a reached surface saying something untrue, fabricating a figure, or certifying what it could not assess. I split the regulatory_map content into its sections and tested each for a scaffold disclosure: every delivered section carries one — "The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them", "Structured risk frame for this dimension - surface, don't invent", "Likelihood x impact to be scored; mitigations and owners to be assigned", "Structured phased frame (deterministic scaffold)", "The native engine flags areas needing attention; a model resource details them". The only two sections without a disclosure phrase are the provenance header line itself ("_[Workstation native structured engine - owned, no external dependency]_") and the Risk Register heading, whose own body then discloses. I also grepped all nine stages for any assertion of the words compliant/verified/validated/certified/halal/permissible: zero hits. And I grepped for fabricated figures (TAM/SAM/SOM, $, %, score) although the prompts explicitly ask for "Market Size Estimate (TAM/SAM/SOM)" and "Urgency and Impact Score (1-10)": the only hits are the two "to be scored" placeholders. Nothing certifies, nothing invents a number. So the content-level honesty is intact and this is not a tier-1 surface.  What remains is exactly the assessor's tier-2 claim: the shortfall is invisible at the level that matters. The run-level surfaces — the done event, the stored entity, the Board vision_summary, the "active" status, the spawned project — carry no §11 verdict and no "this run was not compliance-screened" note, so a reader of the run (or of the entity a week later) cannot tell that nothing screened it, while the sibling route screens the identical text and fails it. Note the route is API-only: Grep for "studio/synthesise", "/api/v1/studio" and "synthesis_studio" across apps/ found no frontend consumer (the /synthesis page is a different module, agentic_core/synthesis/api.py), so the reached surface here is the API response and the stored entity, which is what I examined. That keeps it off tier 1 and squarely on tier 2 — no correction up or down.  One incidental observation, not part of R1.4 and not raised as a finding: at synthesis_studio.py:299-303 the W506 provenance work computes _stage_served and _stage_ext and then never includes them in the yielded "complete" event — dead assignments. It is not a truth defect because the gateway's provenance line is printed inside each stage's content (W570: a printed line is a surface), which I confirmed on both runs.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed POST /api/v1/studio/synthesise TWICE with my OWN challenges, not the assessor's. (1) "build a pork-based protein bar brand financed by a riba interest loan and sold to children" -> 200, 20 SSE events, 9 stages in 21038 ms, stage 6 = "REGULATORY MAP", done event {synthesis_id 8eb367d4aeb5, entity_id vsb-8eb367d4, solution_name "General Solution", stages_completed 9}. (2) "launch a private paediatric vaccine clinic chain handling patient health records" (domain=health, realm=civic) -> 200, 9 stages in 3328 ms, entity vsb-57d7a295. Scanned every event of both streams for any key matching compl|qual|screen|gate|halal|bar|verdict|assur: the ONLY matches are the literal "complete" stage name and "stages_completed". Zero compliance verdict, zero §10 quality record, on either run. Then GET /api/v1/studio/vsb/vsb-8eb367d4 and walked the whole stored entity: entity_id, owner_id, synthesis_id, challenge, solution_name, realm, domain, status="active", created_at, stages{9}, duration_seconds, board{chief,directors[7],governance,vision_summary="Commercialise: build a pork-based protein bar brand financed by a riba interest loan and sold to chi..."}, economy{entity_type "waqf_ltd_hybrid", 6-stage waterfall, currency WST}, living, business_plan_scope. Keys matching compl|qual|screen|gate in the entity: [] (empty). So an "active" waqf-hybrid VSB with a Board charter to COMMERCIALISE a pork+riba+marketed-to-children venture was persisted with no record that it was never screened. Reproduced the grep with Grep on C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\api\synthesis_studio.py for screen_compliance|assure_delivery|bar_measured -> "No matches found", and read the whole 452-line module: the stream() loop calls only gateway.query_meta per stage, then enrich_vsb_entity, _save_vsb, biobus.record_operation("bto_cascade", success=True) and a project _save. There is no call site for any compliance screen or quality gate. Then ran the CONTRAST route with the SAME mission of my own: POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade {"mission":"build a pork-based protein bar brand financed by a riba interest loan and sold to children"} -> 200, quality.compliance.overall="fail", compliant=false, verdicts[0]={framework "sharia_halal", status "fail", reason "haram term present in the text: 'pork' - a keyword screen, not a certification (engine-backed)"}, plus quality.bar_measured over the §10 criteria, quality.qms_gate_passed, quality.quality_record_hash and compliance_routed_to_cca=true. So the machinery fires on MY input one module away, and the flagship cascade never reaches it.

### R1.5 · The Studio stream computes per-stage provenance and throws it away; the code's own basis comment claims it is emitted — **DOC_OVERCLAIM** · tier 2

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The content is self-labelled "_[Workstation native structured engine…]_" inside the markdown, so a reader is not told a model wrote it. But the structured provenance the vision's badge rule depends on is absent while an in-code basis says it is present — a future reader auditing §11 rule 4 would check the comment and move on.
- **claim:** §11 rule 4 — "every AI-served surface renders serving provenance (the shared amber 'structured floor — not model analysis' badge)"
- **observed:** agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py:297-303. The comment reads "an SSE engine carries provenance on EVERY stage event, which the item names explicitly; a reader watching the stream must not have to wait for the final record." Lines 300-301 assign `_stage_served = _st.get("served_by")` and `_stage_ext = bool(_st.get("is_external"))`; line 303's yield is `json.dumps({"stage":"complete","stage_key":…,"stage_label":…,"content":…,"stage_num":…})` — neither variable appears. Both are dead. I parsed the live stream: every `complete` event's key set is exactly ['content','stage','stage_key','stage_label','stage_num'], and the `done` frame is ['stage','synthesis_id','entity_id','solution_name','duration_ms','stages_completed'] — no served_by anywhere in 9 stages.
- **evidence:** Live stage frame: {'stage': 'complete', 'stage_key': 'challenge_analysis', 'stage_label': 'CHALLENGE ANALYSIS', 'stage_num': 1} — against the comment at line 297 asserting provenance on every stage event.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). THE FINDING STANDS, reproduced independently, and the verdict and tier are both correct as assessed — but the overclaim is a layer deeper than the assessor found, and the tier is still 2 for a reason they understated.  WHAT IS TRUE. The provenance is computed and thrown away, exactly as stated. `query_meta` hands back served_by/is_external; lines 300-301 bind them to `_stage_served`/`_stage_ext`; the yield on line 303 names neither; each identifier appears once in the whole file. The basis comment at 295-296 asserts two things and BOTH are false: (a) "an SSE engine carries provenance on EVERY stage event" — my 9 stage frames carry none; and (b) the implicature of "a reader watching the stream must not have to wait for the final record" — which presumes the final record HAS it. It does not. The saved VSB entity is built at lines 307-322 with `"stages": results` where results is {stage_key: content_string}, so the provenance never reaches the durable record either. The assessor checked the stream; I checked the record the comment contrasts the stream against, and the comment's premise fails on that side too.  WHY THE OVERCLAIM IS STRONGER THAN "A CODE COMMENT". The assessor's tier rationale was that "a future reader auditing §11 rule 4 would check the comment and move on." The same untrue clause is in the DELIVERY REGISTER as a closed bar: docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md:1430 reads "P2.2 ✅ DONE W511 … Provenance to every surface. The 47 gateway.query() sites → query_meta carrying {served_by, is_external} (SSE engines: on every stage + complete event)". `/studio/synthesise` is an SSE engine in agentic_core/api and its stage AND complete events carry neither field, so the parenthetical is false of it while the item reads DONE. The item's stated GUARD is only "`gateway.query(` count in agentic_core/api == 0" — a call-site census that is fully green here (this route does call query_meta) and structurally incapable of seeing whether the yielded frame contains the fields. So the guard is vacuous with respect to the clause it is attached to, which is why the gap survived W506 → W511 → now. That is the register-level version of this repo's own lesson that a fix is done only when every writer and an executing guard leg say the new truth.  WHY TIER 2 AND NOT TIER 1. Nothing reached fails to say what served it. The content itself opens with "_[Workstation native structured engine — owned, no external dependency]_" (the marker constant is agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py:24), I saw that string in my own first stage frame, and it is still present in the saved entity record. So the stream discloses the structured floor in prose, no figure is fabricated, and nothing certifies what it could not assess. The missing piece is the MACHINE-READABLE field the amber-badge rule depends on, not the user-facing truth — and no page consumes this route, so there is no screen omitting a badge either. "The model did not run" here is the environment (AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1), and the surface does say so. Tier 1 is not warranted; tier 2 is.  WHY NOT MILDER. One could argue the prose marker satisfies §11 rule 4 and the whole thing is cosmetic. It does not, for a measurable reason: the marker is produced by the native floor and is therefore present only WHEN the floor serves. If an owned or external model served a stage, that stage's content would carry no marker and the frame would carry no served_by, so the stream would be silent about provenance in exactly the case where provenance matters most — the is_external case that the amber badge exists to distinguish. The disclosure currently holds by accident of the serving tier, not by construction. That is a real shortfall, which is why the finding is not refuted or softened; it is simply a documentation-and-plumbing shortfall rather than a lie on a screen.  CORRECTION TO THE ASSESSOR'S EVIDENCE, for the record: their quoted stage frame omitted 'content', which their own key-set line includes. Immaterial to the finding.
- **refuter's evidence:** I ran my OWN cascade, not the assessor's: POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/studio/synthesise with challenge "Rooftop rainwater harvesting for a dense urban mosque campus with no mains irrigation", realm=civic, domain=water, solution_name="Sabil Rain Loop", and parsed all 20 SSE frames. Every one of the 9 `complete` frames had key set exactly ['content','stage','stage_key','stage_label','stage_num']; the `done` frame was ['duration_ms','entity_id','solution_name','stage','stages_completed','synthesis_id']. Needle test over the whole frame blob: 'served_by' False, 'is_external' False. I then went FURTHER than the assessor and fetched the final record the code's own comment points at — GET /api/v1/studio/vsb returned my entity vsb-a6c70e3d, and GET /api/v1/studio/vsb/vsb-a6c70e3d has top keys ['board','business_plan_scope','challenge','created_at','domain','duration_seconds','economy','entity_id','living','owner_id','realm','solution_name','stages','status','synthesis_id'], `stages` is {stage_key: str}, and 'served_by'/'is_external' are absent from the entire serialised record. I read agentic_core/api/synthesis_studio.py:295-303 and grepped the file: `_stage_served` and `_stage_ext` each occur exactly ONCE (the assignment at 300/301) — dead. I confirmed the data exists and is discarded: agentic_core/ai/gateway.py query_meta's docstring states it "returns PROVENANCE — {output, served_by, is_external}", and the sibling `run_lab_cascade` 50 lines above in the SAME file (synthesis_studio.py:249-251) collects `meta.get("served_by")` into a `served` map and returns it. I also grepped every .ts/.tsx/.jsx in the repo for "studio/synthesise": zero hits — the route has no UI consumer (apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/synthesis/SynthesisStudio.tsx consumes a DIFFERENT route, /api/v1/synthesis/stream, and that one does render provenanceBadge / provenanceMapBadge from served_by). Finally I read docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md:1430-1434.

### R1.6 · Two surfaces show the §10 bar as all sixteen criteria and drop the measured/attested/not-measured split the same response carries — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** On a floor-served cascade exactly zero of the sixteen were measured by the gate, and the surface lists all sixteen beside a quality chip without the count that distinguishes them. The honest figure is in the payload the page already holds; two of the four §10 surfaces simply do not show it.
- **claim:** §10 — "specifically designed · modelled · simulated · optimised · categorised · ranked; best-in-class · innovative · effective · safe · efficient · commercially viable · compliant; verified · tested · validated"
- **observed:** agentic_core/vbs/quality.py:140-256 resolves the bar per criterion in three sourced states and returns `bar_measured` with `summary` ("4 measured · 5 attested · 7 not measured"), `measured_criteria`, `attested_criteria` and `screen_only_criteria`. I confirmed the live cascade carries it: POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade returned quality.bar (16 names) AND quality.bar_measured with every criterion's state. Repo-wide grep for `bar_measured` in apps/ matches only pages/Deliverables.tsx:19,225-226 and pages/synthesis/GenesisJourney.tsx:55,841-842. components/organism/SwarmIntelligence.tsx:300 and pages/synthesis/ResourceFabric.tsx:516 both render `§10 quality bar: ${(…quality.bar || []).join(' · ')}` — the sixteen names only — as the tooltip on the QMS chip.
- **evidence:** SwarmIntelligence.tsx:300 — title={`${c.title}\n§10 quality bar: ${(cascade.quality.bar || []).join(' · ')}…`} — while the live response's bar_measured shows "specifically designed" and "verified" both met:null with basis "not assessable — floor-served".
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). STANDS on facts, and my run is starker than theirs: on a floor-served cascade *zero* of sixteen were measured or attested (they cited 4 measured · 5 attested · 7 not measured; the floor-served run I executed reports 0 · 0 · 2 screen-only · 16 not measured). Both named renders really do join quality.bar's sixteen names and really do drop bar_measured, and I proved the split is present in the payload on BOTH pages' own routes, so the gap is a surfacing gap, not a backend gap. The machinery behind §10 is genuinely delivered — three sourced states, screen-only counted out of measured, withheld attestations, floor-served recorded met:null — and two of the four §10 surfaces (Deliverables, GenesisJourney) print the summary plus the measured and attested name lists. PARTIAL is the right verdict and I keep it.  I correct the TIER DOWN 2 -> 3, which is where the assessor went wrong. Their tier rationale is "the surface lists all sixteen beside a quality chip without the count that distinguishes them" — i.e. they treated the chip as carrying no disclosure. It does. The tooltip is `${c.title}\n§10 quality bar: …`, and c.title is qms_basis, so the first line a user reads is: "not assessable — floor-served: the deterministic floor emits the requested headings, so coverage cannot fail by construction and the stub regex never matches its vocabulary; no instrument here can say whether this content is good". The chip's own label on that path is "QMS gate: —", never a pass. On ResourceFabric the adjacent chip on my run read "not commit-ready" (commit_ready came back false). So no reached surface states a met count, fabricates a figure, or certifies what it could not assess — the decisive fact (nothing here can judge this content) is disclosed in the same string, one line above the list, and the list is labelled as the bar (the standard's membership), not as criteria satisfied. Nothing is untrue: the §10 bar IS those sixteen names. Per the tier rule, a real finding whose surface discloses the shortfall is a tier-3 completeness gap, and it is nowhere near tier 1.  The residual defect worth fixing is narrow and genuine: of the sixteen names listed, fourteen are outside the coverage/stub reasoning the basis sentence explains, so a reader could bind "modelled · simulated · optimised · ranked · best-in-class" to work done rather than to criteria no one measured. The fix is one token — append bar_measured.summary to the two tooltips through the same helper that already builds them, rather than at the two call sites, so a third surface cannot repeat it.
- **refuter's evidence:** I reproduced every factual leg with my own inputs, not the assessor's. (1) POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade on 127.0.0.1:8086 with my own mission ("Design a cold-chain logistics audit for a halal dairy cooperative in Yorkshire", realm enterprise, domain agriculture, csuite_roles COO+CFO) -> HTTP 200. The response carries BOTH quality.bar (exactly the sixteen §10 names) AND quality.bar_measured, whose summary on my run is "0 measured · 0 attested · 2 screen-only · 16 not measured", measured_criteria [], attested_criteria [], screen_only_criteria ["compliant","safe"], with qms_gate_passed null and the floor-served basis. (2) I then drove the SECOND accused surface's own route, which the assessor did not: POST /api/v1/resources/compose/simulate with my own composition (resource_ids spi+ddpie, usage_area enterprise) -> HTTP 200; simulation.quality again carries bar (16 names) and bar_measured with the same "0 measured · 0 attested · 2 screen-only · 16 not measured". So the premise "the honest figure is in the payload the page already holds" is true on BOTH pages. (3) Read C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\vbs\quality.py:138-256 (_measure_bar, the three sourced states gate/caller/none plus screen and withheld). (4) Read both renders: C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\apps\workstation-superapp\src\components\organism\SwarmIntelligence.tsx:298-303 and C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\apps\workstation-superapp\src\pages\synthesis\ResourceFabric.tsx:514-519 — both are title={`${c.title}\n§10 quality bar: ${(…quality.bar || []).join(' · ')}`}, the sixteen names only. (5) Repo-wide grep for bar_measured across apps/ matches only pages/Deliverables.tsx:19,224-226,233-236 and pages/synthesis/GenesisJourney.tsx:55,840-842,848-852 — confirming 2 of the 4 §10 surfaces drop the split. (6) The leg the assessor did NOT check: I read qmsChip at C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\apps\workstation-superapp\src\lib\api.ts:256-266 and found c.title IS q.qms_basis, so the first line of the very same tooltip is the not-assessable disclosure. I reconstructed the exact rendered string.

### R1.7 · Synthesis records which selected sources it could not read and surfaces it nowhere — and the guard for it only greps the source text — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A user who attaches three documents the platform cannot extract gets a complete-looking report built from none of them, and the page never says so. The guard that is supposed to hold this honest asserts a variable NAME exists in the file, which it does — so the gap is invisible to the test suite as well as to the reader.
- **claim:** §10 — every solution is "specifically designed"; §11 — solutions are "ethically evaluated for … quality and value"
- **observed:** agentic_core/synthesis/api.py:53-80: files whose `status != "EXTRACTED"` are collected into `skipped`, stored as `self._last_context_skipped` (line 77) and written to a server log (line 78-80). Repo-wide grep for `_last_context_skipped` / `context_skipped` / `sources_skipped` outside _archive returns exactly two hits: the assignment at api.py:77 and integration_tests/test_mvp_spine.py:21361, which asserts `"_last_context_skipped" in _syn` — a substring test over the file's own text. The sibling path does it properly: career.py returns `unread_files` + `unread_basis` and ApplicationStudio.tsx reads both (asserted at test_mvp_spine.py:21363-21366). I executed POST /api/v1/synthesis/generate with content_ids=[] and the response carried only content/metadata/served_by/is_external — no source count and no skipped list.
- **evidence:** api.py:78-80 — logger.warning("synthesis context: %d selected file(s) contributed nothing because the platform has not read them: %s", …) — the only place this fact goes.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The mechanical facts reproduce exactly, so the finding is not refuted: the skipped list is log-only, it reaches neither the /generate response, the /stream done frame, the result card, nor the downloaded file; and the guard that is supposed to hold it honest is a substring assertion over api.py's own source text, which cannot fail on behaviour (it would stay green if line 77 were dead code).  But the finding's title and its tier-2 harm argument both rest on a premise I disproved: "surfaces it nowhere" and "the page never says so". The page does say so, at the point of selection, visibly — not in a tooltip (the W490 class was already applied here at SynthesisStudio.tsx:766-772). Every file chip for a file synthesis will skip carries a "not read" badge, because in_knowledge_base (ingestion/api.py:167) is the exact logical complement of the skip test (synthesis/api.py:69). I checked for the divergence that would have made this a tier-1 untruth — a file badged read but skipped anyway, or a page claiming a source count — and there is none: the two predicates agree by construction, and the page's only count ("N of M selected", line 727) is a selection count, which is true. So the assessor's scenario, a user attaching three unextractable documents, is a user looking at three chips each stamped "not read".  Tier correction down to 3 by the brief's own rule ("if the finding is real but the surface discloses it, correct the tier DOWN"). No reached surface states a falsehood, fabricates a figure, or certifies what it could not assess; the output additionally discloses its own nature hard ("structured floor - not model analysis", in both the card badge and the downloaded file). The residual defect is real but is about disclosure LOCALITY and guard quality: the "not read" fact lives at the selection surface and does not travel with the output the way career.py's unread_files/unread_basis travels to ApplicationStudio.tsx, and the suite's only check is vacuous. Verdict PARTIAL stands — the skipping machinery and the per-file disclosure are real, the output-side statement and a live guard are missing.  One gap neither of us can pin on a surface: a content_id matching no registry entry (a deleted file) is not added to `skipped` at all and so would not even reach the log. It is not user-reachable from this page, since ids come from /ingest/list.
- **refuter's evidence:** Independent inputs, not the assessor's empty content_ids. I uploaded two of my own unreadable probes to POST /api/v1/ingest/ (r17_probe.mp3 -> status NOT_TRANSCRIBED, in_knowledge_base false; r17_probe.pdf -> NOT_EXTRACTED, in_knowledge_base false), then ran POST /api/v1/synthesis/generate with BOTH real file_ids and my own topic ("Groundwater recharge in arid basins"). Response keys were exactly content/is_external/metadata/output_id/output_url/served_by/timestamp — no source count, no skipped list; the 2492-char report body contains none of "skip", "not read", "unread", "not transcribed", or the probe filenames. I then GET /api/v1/synthesis/download/{output_id}: the persisted file carries only the served_by provenance line, nothing about sources. Code read: agentic_core/synthesis/api.py:64-84 (skipped built, assigned to self._last_context_skipped, emitted only to logger.warning), the non-streaming result dict at :364-374 and the /stream done frame at :486-493 — neither carries a skipped field. Grep tool for _last_context_skipped/context_skipped/sources_skipped outside _archive returns the two hits the assessor named. I read the guard at integration_tests/test_mvp_spine.py:21359-21361: it read_text()s api.py and asserts the literal "_last_context_skipped" is in that text — a source-substring test, not a behavioural one. BUT I also read the reached page: apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/synthesis/SynthesisStudio.tsx:769-772 renders a VISIBLE amber "not read" badge (data-testid ingest-unread-{file_id}) on every file chip where f.in_knowledge_base === false, outside the `active` conditional so it shows selected or not; the chips come from GET /api/v1/ingest/list, which I called live and which returned in_knowledge_base false for both of my probes. agentic_core/ingestion/api.py:167 computes in_knowledge_base as (status == "EXTRACTED" and extracted_text.strip()) — the exact complement of synthesis's skip test at api.py:69 (status != "EXTRACTED" or not text), so the badge and the skip rule are the same predicate.

### R1.8 · The faith-content constitution's rules 1–4 hold under execution — **DELIVERED**

- **claim:** §11 rules 1–4 + A.9.1/A.9.2/A.9.3 — Arabic never AI-generated, nonexistent ayaat refused, truncated ranges disclosed, recitation never scored, translation refuses rather than approximating
- **observed:** Four executions. (a) POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir {surah 2, ayah 255} → sourced Arabic with `"arabic_source":"alquran.cloud — authentic sourced text, never AI-generated"`, `sections_withheld:["Transliteration","Translation"]`, and a floor_note stating the notes are a structured frame not scholarship. (b) {surah 2, ayah 300} → HTTP 422 "surah 2 has 286 ayaat — ayah 300 does not exist". (c) {2:280-290} → 200 with `"reference":"Surah 2:280-286"` — the range actually covered, not the range asked for. (d) POST /api/v1/qep/translation/translate with Arabic → HTTP 503 "the deterministic native floor cannot translate and its output will not be presented as a translation". (e) POST /api/v1/qep/tajweed/analyse with real Arabic → text_similarity 0.4286 with scope "TEXT comparison only. This says NOTHING about recitation, pronunciation, makharij, or sifat". GET /api/v1/qep/status confirms "tajweed":"text tools only … NO recitation assessment (no phonetic model is provisioned)". GET /api/qep/analytics/overview returns measured:false with metrics:{} rather than figures.
- **evidence:** "surah 2 has 286 ayaat — ayah 300 does not exist" (HTTP 422) and "reference":"Surah 2:280-286" for a 280-290 request.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). SAME / DELIVERED — rules 1–4 of §11 and A.9.1/A.9.2/A.9.3 hold under my own execution, and crucially the refusal does not merely refuse, it states a CORRECT figure: I picked al-Kawthar specifically because its count (3) is small and checkable, and the route got it right, as did the whole 114-entry table (6236 total). So no tier-1 candidate: no reached surface told me anything untrue, fabricated a figure, or certified what it could not assess. The opposite — the honest-shortfall disclosures are what the surfaces actually carry: the tafsir withholds Translation and Transliteration rather than inventing them, the floor_note calls its own notes a frame and not scholarship, `qms_basis` volunteers "not assessable — floor-served … no instrument here can say whether this content is good", translation 503s rather than approximating, and the tajweed scope line names precisely what the number does not mean. The environment (AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1) is why a model did not translate, and the 503 SAYS so, which is the correct behaviour under the task's own rule.  Two sub-tier-3 imprecisions I found that do not disturb the verdict, logged only for completeness: (a) at `agentic_core/api/religion.py:259-263` the surah-length clamp `ayah_end = min(ayah_end, _AYAH_COUNTS[surah])` runs BEFORE `range_note` is computed, so a surah-overflow truncation emits no note at all — my 114:4-10 came back with `range_note: None`, the only disclosure being the corrected `reference`/`ayah_end`. That is accurate rather than untrue (it never claims to cover 4-10), so it is disclosure-by-correction, not an over-claim. (b) As a consequence, when both clamps fire the note misreports the request: my 2:1-500 returned "covers 2:1-10, not the requested end 286" — I asked for 500, and 286 is the clamped value, not my request. The coverage statement and the reference are both true; only the restatement of my input is wrong, so nothing is overstated about what was delivered.  One transcription slip in the assessor's write-up, not in the system: they report posting `{surah 2, ayah 255}`, but the model field is `ayah_start` (religion.py:223) — that literal body returns 422 "Field required", as I confirmed. The substance of what they observed still reproduces once the field is named correctly, so this does not refute the finding.
- **refuter's evidence:** I executed all five rules against the live backend with my own inputs, never the assessor's. (1) Arabic never AI-generated — POST /api/v1/religion/quran-tafsir {surah 36, ayah_start 9, ayah_end 9} (NOT their 2:255) → 200 with the sourced Arabic of 36:9, `"arabic_source":"alquran.cloud — authentic sourced text, never AI-generated"`, `sections_withheld:["Transliteration","Translation"]`, and a floor_note saying the notes "are a structured frame, not scholarship"; the tafsir body itself states the Arabic "is shown to the reader above these notes and is not repeated in them" and that its bullet list "is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them". (2) Nonexistent ayah — I chose Surah 108 al-Kawthar (3 ayaat) and asked for ayah 7 → HTTP 422 `"surah 108 has 3 ayaat — ayah 7 does not exist"`. (3) Truncated range — I chose 114:4-10 (an-Nas has 6) → 200 with `"reference":"Surah 114:4-6"` and ayah_end corrected to 6; and 2:10-40 → `"reference":"Surah 2:10-19"` with an explicit `range_note`: "range capped at 10 ayaat per request — this tafsir covers 2:10-19, not the requested end 40". (4) Translation — POST /api/v1/qep/translation/translate with 112:1 Arabic (NOT their input) → HTTP 503 "no model resource is available to translate — the deterministic native floor cannot translate and its output will not be presented as a translation". (5) Recitation never scored — POST /api/v1/qep/tajweed/analyse with 112:1 against a learner typo → text_similarity 0.375 under scope "TEXT comparison only. This says NOTHING about recitation, pronunciation, makharij, or sifat", plus a top-level disclaimer; a perfect match → 1.0 with the same scope. I also ran the adversarial W439 shape myself (English prose "my recitation had excellent makharij" as recited_text) → `comparable:false`, "nothing is scored that cannot be compared" — no accuracy, confidence, makharij or score key anywhere in the payload. Finally I verified the figure rule 2 states is a true figure: `agentic_core/religious_domain/api.py` `_AYAH_COUNTS` sums to 6236 (the canonical Hafs total) and matched 20/20 surah counts I checked independently (1:7, 2:286, 3:200, 4:176, 9:129, 17:111, 18:110, 19:98, 24:64, 36:83, 55:78, 67:30, 78:40, 97:5, 103:3, 108:3, 110:3, 112:4, 113:5, 114:6).

### R1.9 · The §11 federated screen and the §10 bar both refuse to clear what nothing assessed — **DELIVERED**

- **claim:** §11 — "Continuously monitored and evaluated live compliance to legal, regulatory, international, and industry best-practice standards"; §10 — solutions are "compliant · verified · tested · validated"
- **observed:** POST /api/v1/compliance/check {"subject":"a new pharmaceutical manufacturing line in London"} → overall "review", compliant **null**, `coverage_gaps:["constitutional","ehs","ethical","sharia_halal","uk_legal"]`, `assessed_by:[]`, `vocabulary_only:["regulatory"]`, with per-row reasons naming what each engine could and could not do. The riba cascade returned sharia_halal "fail" on the term with coverage "vocabulary". agentic_core/vbs/quality.py:200-226 makes 'compliant' and 'safe' met=True only when every in-scope row passed AND each could assess (coverage 'engine'), met=None otherwise with source 'screen'; lines 184-187 mark 'specifically designed' and 'verified' not-assessable on floor-served content. GET /api/v1/operations/rankings reports success_rate null with "NOT ASSESSED — no quality gate evaluated any of this resource's 1 run(s); 1 produced output, which is not the same thing". GET /api/v1/gaas/status declines to aggregate: "This is not a platform-wide constitutional health figure." UEG shows compliance.screen × 28 real events.
- **evidence:** "A screen can refuse and can escalate; it cannot clear — so only a row with coverage 'engine' can carry a pass, and `coverage_gaps` names every area nothing here assessed" — with compliant:null on a subject every framework returned 'review' for.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding reproduces exactly as assessed, and it reproduces on inputs the assessor never used, which is the stronger test. The rule is enforced in code, not asserted in prose: compliance.py:180-184 assessed() requires coverage in ASSESSING_COVERAGE *and* a status that is not not_assessed/not_checked/error, and _coverage_report sets compliant True only when overall=="pass" and gaps is empty and can is non-empty — and because the constitutional row is constructed with coverage "none"/"screen" or status not_checked/not_assessed/error on every branch (compliance.py:599-605), gaps is never empty on /check, so a "compliant: true" from that route is structurally unreachable. That is the conservative direction, not a defect. quality.py:200-226 mirrors the same rule for the §10 bar with a third state (source "screen") rather than a pass, and 184-187 refuses "specifically designed"/"verified" on floor-served content. My own clean subject — one a naive implementation would have waved through, since nothing failed — came back compliant null on both the §11 route and the §10 bar, with every unassessed area named. No reached surface clears, certifies, or fabricates: the page (ComplianceChecker.tsx:84-97) gates its emerald state and its check icon on assessed_by being non-empty and prints "NOTHING here assessed this subject. A keyword screen can refuse a subject; it cannot clear one." plus the gap list, and complianceChip (lib/api.ts:195-207) greys a "pass" whose assessed_by is empty. So there is no tier-1 defect here: DELIVERED stands, direction SAME.  One rider, which I am deliberately NOT inflating. The basis sentence in compliance.py:228-234 hard-codes its tail: when `can` is empty it appends "— NOTHING here assessed this subject, so the overall is 'review' and no part of it may be reported as compliant" regardless of the actual overall. On my riba/asbestos subject and on deliv-ee6281e6 the overall was "fail" and compliant false, yet the basis string said the overall is 'review'. It is an untrue sentence about its own result, but it understates severity rather than clearing anything, the same payload carries overall:"fail" and compliant:false one field away, and no page renders `basis` verbatim (the checker page renders assessed_by/coverage_gaps instead) — it reaches only the API payload, quality["compliance"]["basis"] and the DCMS-sealed record. That is a tier-3 wording defect on a non-rendered field, not a tier-1 false claim, and it does not touch the clause R1.9 is about. Second rider, no defect: my "asbestos insulation" subject still returned ehs "not_assessed — keyword screen found no hazard term", i.e. the EHS word list does not contain asbestos; the row says plainly that nothing assessed the subject, so the shortfall is disclosed rather than hidden. The tier I report (3) is for that rider; the finding itself carries no defect tier.
- **refuter's evidence:** Reproduced on three subjects I chose myself plus the assessor's one. (1) POST /api/v1/compliance/check {"subject":"an interest-bearing bond issuance with riba and gharar, using asbestos insulation in the plant"} -> overall "fail", compliant false, sharia_halal fail coverage "vocabulary", coverage_gaps ["constitutional","ehs","ethical","regulatory","uk_legal"], assessed_by [], vocabulary_only ["sharia_halal"]. (2) Same route, {"subject":"a halal-certified zakat-funded community garden water fountain"} -> overall "review", compliant **null**, sharia_halal "review" with the reason "that is the subject's own claim, not a certification ... a halal verdict comes from a certifying body", assessed_by []. (3) Drove the §10 bar for real, not by reading it: POST /api/v1/deliverables/produce with my own 3-section cold-chain depot brief containing "interest-bearing" and "ammonia" -> deliv-ee6281e6, quality_assurance.quality.bar_measured.criteria.compliant {met:false, measured:true, source:"gate", basis:"§11 screen verdicts: {...} — a §11 row failed"}, safe likewise false. (4) A second, deliberately clean deliverable (a neighbourhood tool library, no trigger vocabulary) -> compliant {met:null, measured:false, source:"screen", basis:"... not established: ehs, ethical, regulatory, sharia_halal, uk_legal could not assess this subject (a keyword screen can refuse, not clear)"}, safe the same, while "specifically designed" and "verified" were met:true from real coverage 1.0 / QMS gate, and the counts line read "2 measured · 0 attested · 2 screen-only · 14 not measured" with screen_only_criteria ["compliant","safe"]. Read agentic_core/vbs/quality.py:200-226 (_bar_from_rows) and agentic_core/api/compliance.py:180-234 (assessed(), _coverage_report). Also executed GET /api/v1/operations/rankings (success_rate null, success_basis "NOT ASSESSED — no quality gate evaluated any of this resource's N run(s); N produced output, which is not the same thing" on every row) and GET /api/v1/gaas/status (circuit_breaker_scope ends "This is not a platform-wide constitutional health figure."; UEG total_events 704 with compliance.screen 114).
