### R3.2 · The AI CEO surface prints a "## Board directives" section containing three invented bullets while the same card states it was grounded in 0 directives — **PARTIAL** *(assessed STUB)* · tier 1

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Not that a model did not run — that is the floor. The defect is that one card simultaneously states there are zero board directives and prints three under that exact heading. A provenance badge says who served the text; it does not say the factual section headings in the body were filled with content the engine did not derive.
- **claim:** §5: "**AI CEO** — receives the action plan; integrates the **living management systems (BMS · QMS · DCS · EMS)**; delegates to the specialist C-Suite." (the Chief→Board→AI CEO chain)
- **observed:** Executed POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v138/ceo/chat with {"message":"What are the board directives?"}. The streamed answer's first section is headed "## Board directives" and carries three generic bullets; the final SSE event reports grounding.directives = 0. agentic_core/api/v138/ceo.py:365 proves the grounding text handed in was "## Board directives\n- none recorded for this scope". Re-ran after recording a REAL directive (POST /api/v1/board/chief/instruct → dir-cb446ce1, "Prioritise the Education domain this quarter"): grounding.directives = 1 but the answer printed the SAME three boilerplate bullets, not the directive. Reader: apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/CEOChat.tsx:288 prints "grounded in 0 directives · 0 objectives · scope workstation" beside that answer; the provenance badge (:285) names the server, not the body.
- **evidence:** Answer body: "## Board directives\n- Validate the structured outputs above against the stated objective.\n- Route richer generation to a model resource (local-first; external only if enabled).\n- Iterate via the orchestrator's cascade for the stated domain; record outcomes to memory/UEG."  Final event: {"done": true, "served_by": "native", "grounding": {"scope": "workstation", "directives": 0, …}}
- **refutation: STOOD, verdict CORRECTED STUB → PARTIAL.** STANDS, reproduced twice with my own inputs, including the 0-directive state the assessor saw and a 1-directive state they only sampled once.  TIER STAYS 1. This is not "a model did not run". The tier-1 bar is a reached surface telling a user something untrue, and one card does exactly that in two ways at once: it states there are zero board directives and, three lines up, prints three imperative bullets under the heading "## Board directives". A user who asks what the board has directed is handed three instructions no board issued, in the answer's first and most authoritative section. The contradiction is on one card, so the counter is not a disclosure — it is a second claim the user has to adjudicate against the first.  The amber badge is real mitigation and I weighed it, but it does not reach this defect. "structured floor — not model analysis" discloses the body's ORIGIN (a deterministic engine, not inference). It does not say that a heading naming a real store was filled with content unrelated to that store — a deterministic engine could perfectly well list stored directives verbatim, which is what the grounding text at ceo.py:365 was built to let it do. The asymmetry inside the engine is the clinching evidence: the seven sections that are obviously framing all carry "not findings about them", and the four that name real stores (board directives, living plan, business plan, next actions) carry no caveat at all. The sections most readable as fact are exactly the ones with the disclosure missing.  VERDICT CORRECTED MILDER, STUB → PARTIAL. The §5 grounding chain is not a stub: _ceo_grounding really reads the board store (it moved 0 → 1 when a directive was recorded, and my VSB scope returned a true objectives=3 and debate_entries=6), the counter it surfaces is honest, and the scope gate at ceo.py:464-471 genuinely 404s an unknown scope. What is missing is the last hop — the generation floor never consumes the grounding it was handed. So this is real machinery with a surface that misreports, not an absent one. The direction label is MILDER because of the verdict word only; the M1 tier-1 count is unchanged at 1, and the fix is narrow: either suppress a store-named heading the floor cannot fill from the grounding, or make _plan()'s output carry the same "not findings" caveat its sibling branches already carry.
- **refuter's evidence:** Two runs of my own against the live backend, plus the code path.  (1) MY OWN SCOPE AND QUESTION — exact reproduction of the assessor's premise with a different subject. POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v138/ceo/chat with {"message":"List the board directives governing this venture and nothing else.","scope":"vsb-1b261cdf1e"} (a VSB I picked myself from GET /api/v1/vsb, not the assessor's scope). Reassembled body begins:   "## Board directives    - Validate the structured outputs above against the stated objective.    - Route richer generation to a model resource (local-first; external only if enabled).    - Iterate via the orchestrator's cascade for the stated domain; record outcomes to memory/UEG." Final event: {"done":true,"served_by":"native","grounding":{"scope":"vsb-1b261cdf1e","directives":0,"objectives":3,"plan_score":0.3,"debate_entries":6}}. So: zero directives for that scope, three bullets printed under that exact heading, on the same response.  (2) MY OWN QUESTION AT WORKSTATION SCOPE — the second leg. {"message":"Summarise the current board directives and which domain I should prioritise."} returned grounding.directives = 1 (the real directive dir-cb446ce1, "Prioritise the Education domain this quarter", is still in the board store), and the body printed THE SAME three bullets. The real directive's text appears nowhere in the answer. So the body is identical whether the store holds 0 or 1 directives — it is not derived from the grounding at all.  (3) THE MECHANISM, read in source. agentic_core/api/v138/ceo.py:347-396 (_ceo_grounding) genuinely reads the board store, counts rows, scores the living plan, loads the scope's business plan and the real meeting log, and at :365 hands in "## Board directives\n- none recorded for this scope" when empty. The generation floor then discards those bodies and keeps only the headings: agentic_core/ai/native/engine.py:296 — has("next","step","action","plan","recommend","directive",...) matches the heading word "directive"/"plan", so the section is filled by _plan() at engine.py:315-318, which is the three literal bullets. That branch is why exactly four sections — Board directives, Living plan, Business plan for this scope, Next actions — print bare imperatives, while the other seven sections (Owner Intent, Understanding, Recent C-Suite debate, Question, Assessment, Priorities, Terms most frequent) each carry the engine's own caveat "the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them" (engine.py:302-305).  (4) THE READER. apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/CEOChat.tsx:288 renders "grounded in 0 directives · 3 objectives · scope vsb-1b261cdf1e" beside that answer; :285 renders provenanceBadge("native") which resolves at apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/api.ts:91 to label "structured floor — not model analysis", title "the deterministic native floor composes structured output from the request — it is not model inference".

### R3.3 · The org cascade's governance chip tooltip tells the reader the delivery's content was NOT screened, while the same response says it screened 18,368 characters across all eight tiers — **DOC_OVERCLAIM** · tier 2 *(assessed tier 1)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A reached tooltip states a falsehood about what the arms-length gate did over the whole §5 delivery, and the reader cannot tell which of two contradictory sentences in the same object is true. The W505/FU-006 fix added the true fields and left the false one in place — a field's meaning belongs to its readers.
- **claim:** §5: "**Change Control (arms-length Agency)** — governs all change at arm's length from operations."
- **observed:** Executed POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/swarm/cascade. The governance object carries BOTH the stale constant and the live facts. agentic_core/gaas/v5/__init__.py:24-26 defines INTENT_GATE_SCOPE (the "NOT screened" sentence) and :32 puts it in every result as `scope`; agentic_core/api/swarm.py:980 builds the result from that helper and :982-986 update()s screened/content_screened/content_chars/tiers_screened — but never overwrites `scope`. Reader: apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/organism/SwarmIntelligence.tsx:285 sets title={cascade.governance.scope}, and the chip body at :286-287 appends "· content not screened" only when content_screened === false, so the chip reads "intent gate: allowed" with a tooltip that contradicts it.
- **evidence:** "governance": {"status":"allowed", "scope":"intent + domain only — … The delivery's content was NOT screened, so this verdict is not a judgement on what was produced.", "screened":"intent + domain + the delivered content of every tier", "content_screened":true, "content_chars":18368, "tiers_screened":["Chief","Board of Directors","AI CEO","C-Suite: CTO","CoE: CTO CoE","CoE: Delivery","Business Transformation Office","Build-to-Order"]}
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The defect is real and I reproduced it on my own journey: one response object states both "content was NOT screened" and "38,778 characters of every tier were screened", and the one field the page puts in front of a user is the false one. The W505/FU-006 fix added the true fields and left the stale constant in place — the field's meaning changed for its reader without the reader being updated.  But the tier is wrong. Tier 1 is for a reached surface that fabricates a figure, certifies what it could not assess, or induces unearned trust. This one errs in the opposite direction on every count: the §5 arms-length gate genuinely ran over the whole delivery (38,778 chars, 15 tiers, violations list carried), no figure is invented, and the stale sentence UNDER-claims what the gate did. Nothing on the surface tells a reader that something unassessed was assessed; it tells them something assessed was not. The chip's visible text ("intent gate: allowed") is accurate, and the suppression logic at :287 correctly withholds "· content not screened" — the falsehood lives only in the hover tooltip, and the same object hands any API consumer the four true fields next to it. So the surface does contradict itself rather than conceal, which the brief says should pull the tier down.  Verdict label stays DOC_OVERCLAIM as the closest fit in the taxonomy (a claim sentence on a surface that does not match the state), with the correction that it is a stale under-claim, not an over-claim: the §5 machinery is delivered and the disclosure text is the defect. Fix shape, for the record: override `scope` at swarm.py:981-995 with a sentence that matches the live facts (the content WAS screened, and by a deterministic pattern screen only), and keep INTENT_GATE_SCOPE intact for the five emitters where it is still true — plus a guard asserting the property (scope must not say "NOT screened" when content_screened is True) rather than the spelling.
- **refuter's evidence:** Reproduced with my OWN input, not the assessor's: POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/swarm/cascade with {"mission":"Stand up a halal-certified cold-chain logistics arm for a regional dairy co-operative","domain":"supply_chain"} → 200. The governance object came back self-contradictory in exactly the shape claimed: scope = "intent + domain only — … The delivery's content was NOT screened, so this verdict is not a judgement on what was produced." alongside screened = "intent + domain + the delivered content of every tier", content_screened = true, content_chars = 38778, tiers_screened = 15 tiers (Chief, Board of Directors, AI CEO, five C-Suite, five CoE, BTO, Build-to-Order), content_compliant true, content_violations []. Read the mechanism: C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\gaas\v5\__init__.py:24-26 defines INTENT_GATE_SCOPE and :32 hard-codes it as `scope` in every intent_gate_result; C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\api\swarm.py:980 builds `governance` from that helper and :981-995 update() screened/content_screened/content_chars/tiers_screened/content_compliant — and never touches `scope`. Grepped the writers: `"content_screened": True` occurs exactly once repo-wide (swarm.py:983), i.e. the cascade is the single call site where the constant becomes false, so the constant is still TRUE for the other emitters and must not simply be deleted. Reader confirmed at C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\apps\workstation-superapp\src\components\organism\SwarmIntelligence.tsx:283-288: title={cascade.governance.scope}, chip body appends "· content not screened" only when content_screened === false, so the rendered chip reads "intent gate: allowed" with a hover tooltip asserting the content was not screened. Checked for a second surface: no download/export of the cascade JSON exists in that component (no createObjectURL/download), so the tooltip plus the raw API response are the only carriers.

### R3.4 · The Strategic and Action-Plan cadence layers of the Living Business System do not exist — nothing refreshes them quarterly, weekly, on market signal or on a KPI — **MISSING** · tier 1 *(assessed tier 3)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** No surface claims a cadence, so nothing untrue is printed about it; the capability is simply absent. Executed GET /api/v1/business-plan: every opening field is "", aims [], roadmap phases []. The only artefact naming the four layers is the per-VSB board pack (vsb.py:1256-1271), assembled on demand from pre-existing fields.

- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding's substance is correct and I reproduced it: nothing in agentic_core refreshes a Strategic or Action-Plan layer quarterly, weekly, on market signal, or on a KPI. The capability is absent. MISSING is the right verdict.  The TIER is wrong, and wrong in the direction that matters for M1. The assessor's tier-3 justification has two load-bearing claims and both are false:  (1) "the one place a reader could have learned the layers are hollow is the basis string R3.1 shows is unreachable." I reached it. POST /api/v1/vsb/{vsb_id}/board-pack followed by GET of the same path returns the `layers` object with every `basis` string populated. It is a reached surface.  (2) "No surface claims a cadence, so nothing untrue is printed about it." The reached surface prints something untrue about these very two layers. On both of my VSBs the strategic layer is certified `present: true` with the basis "the AI CEO specification recorded on this entity" — while the thing recorded on the entity is the placeholder string "content pending the owned model — this enterprise has not yet composed its own CEO specification". And `layers_present` lists "strategic" among the present layers. A reader of the board pack is told the strategic layer exists and that a CEO specification backs it, when the stored content says in words that none was ever composed. That is a reached surface certifying what it could not assess — tier 1.  This is NOT the AI_DISABLE_LOCAL environment excusing itself. The layer's own `ceo` content DOES disclose the pending state; it is the three provenance fields built on top of it (`present`, `basis`, `layers_present`) that contradict the content they summarise. A surface that carries the disclosure in the body and the opposite claim in its own presence field has failed to say it.  The cause is a type confusion in the W496 fix itself, at C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\api\vsb.py:1256-1265:     _ceo_spec = vsb.get("ceo_specification") or {}     "strategic": {"ceo": _ceo_spec, "present": bool(_ceo_spec), ...} `ceo_specification` is written as a STRING at every writer I found — genesis.py:1129 and :1340 (`(req.commercialisation or req.concept)[:2000]`) and vsb.py:1969 (`ceo_spec[:2000]`) — so the `or {}` dict-default never fires and `bool()` of the non-empty placeholder is True. The W496 comment says the layer "was `{"ceo": {}}` on every entity whose CEO specification had not been composed": the author assumed a dict. The pending state is a truthy string, so the fix made the chip report PRESENT on exactly the entities it was written to catch.  This is the "fix every writer, not one" class. The SAME FILE already knows the correct test twice: vsb.py:249 gates a review with `any("content pending the owned model" in b for b in body)`, and vsb.py:1899 does `ceo_spec.startswith("content pending the owned model")` for the spawn stream — which is why my spawn correctly streamed "CEO Strategy Pending". The board pack is the one reader that never applies it, so the spawn stream tells the truth and the board pack, the durable artefact a board reads, does not.  Scope note, kept honest: `action_plan` is NOT defective. Its basis on both my VSBs reads "the entity's standing board roster - a roster, not a plan of action; no action items are recorded here" — an accurate disclosure. Only the strategic half carries the untrue claim, but it carries it on the single artefact the finding itself identifies as the only place naming the four layers, and `layers_present` propagates it into a summary list.  Direction HARSHER: tier 3 → tier 1. The assessor was too kind because they believed no surface spoke about these layers; one does, on demand, for every entity, and what it says about the strategic layer is false.
- **refuter's evidence:** I reproduced the premise with my own searches and routes, then reproduced the layer surface on TWO VSBs I spawned myself.  PREMISE (reproduced, my own greps): `grep -rniE "market_signal|market signal|kpi_trigger|on_kpi|signal_trigger"` over agentic_core returns ONE hit, `governance/command_dispatch.py:49 self.mission_kpis = {...}` — a dict of two floats, not a trigger. `grep -rniE "weekly|APScheduler|cron|schedule\("` with prompt/heading lines excluded returns five hits, all unrelated (`management_systems.generate_audit_schedule`, `care/scoring.py` clinical text, `plan_followups` comments, `religious_domain.hifz_schedule`). No scheduler, no market-signal listener, no KPI trigger exists. GET /api/v1/heartbeat/status (my own call) returned `"beats":22` with every recorded beat's `actions` being exactly `["pulse","homeostasis","genome_scan","transformation_tick"]` — not one is a plan-layer refresh — and all five duty levers false. So the cadence capability is genuinely ABSENT. MISSING stands.  STALE EVIDENCE IN THE FINDING: their GET /api/v1/business-plan is no longer the state. Mine returned `objectives` with one row (`obj-b753480f`, "Prioritise the Education domain this quarter", `source:"chief_instruct_fallback"`), `updated_at:"2026-10-04T04:26:23Z"` (not null), and a populated `chief_draft`. The opening prose fields are still "". This does not rescue the finding — it sharpens that no CADENCE wrote those rows; a one-off chief-instruct fallback did.  THE TIER CORRECTION — I executed the surface they called unreachable. ### R3.5 · The Constitutional layer is not genome-locked and has no Ethical mandate — the genome holds none of the four constitutional fields, while the pack reports genome_present beside derived ones — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Real machinery (derived fields with per-field sources, a real genome store) that is incomplete — one of the four named elements is missing entirely and none is locked to the genome — and the surface says neither. The per-field sources stop short of saying "this layer is not genome-locked".
- **claim:** §17.3: "**Constitutional** (genome-locked): Mission · Vision · Values · Ethical mandate (rarely changes)."
- **observed:** Executed GET http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/vsb/vsb-078e6b3af2/genome. genome_spec carries problem/domain/realm/concept/design/commercialisation and constitutional_alignment: null — no mission, no vision, no values, no ethical mandate; epigenetic_traits is {domain, origin}. The board pack's constitutional layer (agentic_core/api/vsb.py:1168-1172) therefore derives mission as f"Deliver: {challenge}", vision as the challenge verbatim, values as one constant string, and emits genome_present: bool(vsb.get("genome_spec")) — true — in the same object. There is no ethical-mandate key at all: the fourth constitutional element is absent, not derived. The three *_source strings honestly disclaim authorship, but no field says the layer is not genome-locked, and R3.1 shows the layer's basis/sources do not reach the page's chip.
- **evidence:** GET /api/v1/vsb/vsb-078e6b3af2/genome → {"genome_spec":{… "constitutional_alignment":null, "constitutional_gate":{…}}, "epigenetic_traits":{"domain":"agriculture","origin":"genesis"}}; vsb.py:1172 `"genome_present": bool(vsb.get("genome_spec")), "entity": vsb_id`
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Every factual element of the finding reproduces on my own entity and holds for all three generation paths, so it is not REFUTED and PARTIAL is the right verdict: this is real machinery (a real genome store, a real derived layer with per-field sources) that is incomplete against §17.3 — three of four elements are derived rather than authored, the fourth (Ethical mandate) is absent from the entire codebase, and nothing is locked to the genome.  I correct the tier DOWN from 2 to 3, so this is MILDER. Two of the assessor's tier-2 grounds do not survive reading the surface with my own pack:  - "no field says the layer is not genome-locked" is over-stated. The narrative that the page actually prints positively names the real source — "the constitutional layer is DERIVED from the founder's problem statement and the platform's standing values line". Naming the true origin is the disclosure; a reader is told the layer came from the problem statement and a standing values line, not from the genome. - The genome_present: true beside derived fields cannot mislead a page reader, because the page prints neither genome_present nor the derived fields. And the value is literally true: a genome_spec does exist. It is a badly-placed field in an API payload, not a false statement on a surface.  What is genuinely undisclosed is narrower than the assessor's framing: the absence of the Ethical mandate. But no surface claims four constitutional elements exist, no surface claims the layer is genome-locked (that claim lives only in the vision documents, which are the measure, not a user surface), and the layers_present chip's "constitutional" is true — the layer does hold something. So there is no reached surface telling a user something untrue, fabricating a figure, or certifying what it could not assess; tier 1 is clearly wrong here. What remains is a vision-fidelity shortfall against §17.3 whose derivation is disclosed on the surface that shows it, with one named element missing and unmentioned — tier 3.  Caveat I will state plainly: this is a near-boundary 2/3 call. The single thing keeping it off tier 2 is the narrative's positive statement of the real source, and that narrative is the native/pending text. Under AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1 it is what every reader gets, but if a model ever serves the narrative (sb != "native"), that sentence is replaced by model prose and the page would then show a "constitutional" layer with no derivation disclosure at all — at which point this becomes tier 2 again. ### R3.8 · §17.4 Mode 3 human review gates genuinely gate — VERIFIED by driving every lifecycle mover to a 409 and then clearing it — **DELIVERED**

- **claim:** §17.4: "**Mode 3** — optional human review gates at any Concept→Commercialisation stage (set in the VSB genome)."
- **observed:** Executed POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/vsb/vsb-078e6b3af2/review-gates {"stages":["build"]} → the build row came back gated/pending/blocks_progress true. Then POST .../evolve → HTTP 409; POST .../repo/ship → HTTP 409; POST .../board-pack → HTTP 409. Then POST .../review-gates/build/decision {"decision":"approve"} → status approved, blocks_progress false, with a DCS hash on the decision. Restored the VSB by POSTing {"stages":[]}. The on-page copy at apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/synthesis/GenesisJourney.tsx:1197 names exactly what a gate does and does not over-claim mid-journey pausing.
- **evidence:** evolve: {"detail":{"error":"review gate blocks progress — a human review is pending (Mode 3); evolve refused","mover":"evolve","gate":"build","status":"pending","blocks_progress":true,"clear_by":"POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-078e6b3af2/review-gates/build/decision {\"decision\": \"approve\"} — or ungate the stage"}} HTTP 409; decision: {"stage":"build","status":"approved","blocks_progress":false,"dcs_hash":"1cb6feaa07c385f0…"}
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding STANDS and is in fact under-stated as evidence, though the verdict DELIVERED is correct, so the direction is SAME.  Mode 3 genuinely gates. Every lifecycle mover I could reach refused with a 409 that names the gate, its status, the blocking list and the exact route to clear it; clearing it with an approve let every one of them through again; and crucially the ungated baseline was 200 across the board first, so the 409s are caused by MY gate and not by some ambient failure. The guard is one shared function (_refuse_gated) with eleven call sites inside vsb.py and five more modules importing it, so this is not a single route wired by hand.  Two things I verified that the assessor did not, both of which strengthen it rather than weaken it: a REJECTED gate also holds (blocks_progress true, movers 409) — the one state a "decisions are merely recorded" implementation would have let through; and the on-page copy's claims about "plan orchestration" and "cascades" are both executable, returning 409 from business-plan orchestrate and swarm cascade. So the copy at GenesisJourney.tsx:1197 does not over-claim: I could not find a mover it names that does not refuse.  No tier-1 condition is present. There is no reached surface here telling a user something untrue, no fabricated figure, and no certification of what was not assessed — the 409 body is derived entirely from stored state (stages + decisions), and the decision and the config each carry a DCS hash from qms.control_document.  One adjacent observation that does NOT change this verdict, offered for the register rather than as a correction: while my gate was pending and blocking, GET /api/v1/vsb/vsb-52bcb2e537 and the list row both reported status "body pending" with basis "1 body section(s) still await the owned model: ceo_specification", not "held". _derived_status (vsb.py:1406) checks _gates_blocking FIRST and would return ("held", "a review gate blocks progress (Mode 3): validate pending"), but it has a single caller (vsb.py:1988), so the served status is the one written at establish time and is not recomputed on read. The printed sentence is still TRUE (that body section really is pending) and the raw review_gates object is served alongside it, and the gate's own surfaces report pending/blocks_progress correctly — so this is at most a tier-3 precedence staleness, not an untrue statement, and it is a different subject from R3.8.  A second mechanism trap worth recording, which briefly produced a false 200 in my own run: re-POSTing the SAME stage list preserves that stage's prior decision (set_review_gates:1570 keeps decisions for stages still gated), so re-gating an already-approved stage leaves it approved rather than re-arming it. The route's docstring states exactly that ("Decisions for stages no longer gated are cleared"), and the UI's toggle writes the stage off before on, which does clear it — so it is disclosed and not a defect, but any re-test must clear with {"stages":[]} first or it will mistakenly conclude the gate does not hold.
- **refuter's evidence:** Spawned my OWN VSB (vsb-52bcb2e537, "R3.8 Independent Gate Probe", Agriculture, Siwa date-store waqf challenge) and gated my OWN stage — "validate", not the assessor's "build".  (1) BASELINE, no gate — proved red was reachable: POST evolve, repo/ship, board-pack, website, webapp, mobile, repo/cascade, evolution/apply, repo all returned HTTP 200. (2) POST /api/v1/vsb/vsb-52bcb2e537/review-gates {"stages":["validate"]} -> 200, statuses show validate gated/pending/blocks_progress true, dcs_hash c72c8671aaf8… (3) RE-RAN ALL NINE movers -> every one HTTP 409 with the gate named, e.g. {"error":"review gate blocks progress — a human review is pending (Mode 3); phone app refused","mover":"phone app","gate":"validate","status":"pending","blocks_progress":true,"blocking":[{"stage":"validate","status":"pending"}],"clear_by":"POST …/review-gates/validate/decision {\"decision\": \"approve\"} — or ungate the stage"}. That is six movers MORE than the assessor drove (they drove evolve, repo/ship, board-pack). (4) The REJECT state, which the assessor never tested: POST …/review-gates/validate/decision {"decision":"reject"} -> status "rejected", blocks_progress TRUE, dcs c61b7a4e11c4…; evolve and repo/ship still 409. A rejected gate holds, not just a pending one. (5) APPROVE -> {"stage":"validate","status":"approved","blocks_progress":false,"dcs_hash":"60110c3d63f7…"}; all nine movers back to 200. (6) Tested the on-page copy's TWO claims the assessor did not: "plan orchestration" and cascades. POST /api/v1/business-plan/objective/obj-b2efa8ab/orchestrate {"scope":"vsb-52bcb2e537"} -> 409 mover "orchestrate"; POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade {"scope":"vsb-52bcb2e537"} -> 409 mover "cascade". Enforcement lives in four more modules than vsb.py: business_plan.py:397, swarm.py:363, resource_fabric.py:1758, economy.py:1314, plus heartbeat.py:518/574 for the autonomous beat. (7) Read the code: C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\api\vsb.py — _gate_status:1374, _gates_blocking:1387, _refuse_gated:1442 (raises the 409), called at lines 355, 598, 864, 1002, 1153, 2028, 2132, 2192, 2549. set_review_gates:1560 validates stage ids against _LIFECYCLE_STAGE_IDS (I confirmed the eight: intake, research, design, build, validate, commercialise, genome, launch). ### R3.9 · §5's appraise-and-develop loop genuinely closes cycle-over-cycle — VERIFIED across two consecutive cascades — **DELIVERED**

- **claim:** §5: "The chain (each tier manages, appraises, and develops the tier below; **arms-length** — lower tiers cannot instruct the Board/genome)"
- **observed:** Executed POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/swarm/cascade twice with the same mission. Run 1 (run_id 229ebe0054) produced six appraisals (chief_appraises_board, board_appraises_ceo, ceo_appraises_csuite, csuite_appraises_coe, ceo_appraises_bto, bto_appraises_build) each ending in a "## Development Action", with development_applied {} and tier_identity_applied {} — correct for a first run. Run 2 applied all six, keyed to run 1's id, and each tier carried a persistent identity count. agentic_core/api/swarm.py:428-443 is the mechanism (_dev_for reads the prior cycle's action out of tier_development.json and injects it into the tier's prompt). All seven §5 tiers ran (org_hierarchy Chief → Board → AI CEO → C-Suite → CoE → BTO → Build-to-Order), each engaged officer drove its own CoE (level_2_csuite CTO → level_3_coe "CTO CoE" + "Delivery"), and the AI CEO's four key decisions were document-controlled through the QMS-owned DCMS.
- **evidence:** Run 2: "development_applied": {"chief_appraises_board":"229ebe0054","board_appraises_ceo":"229ebe0054","ceo_appraises_csuite":"229ebe0054","csuite_appraises_coe":"229ebe0054","ceo_appraises_bto":"229ebe0054","bto_appraises_build":"229ebe0054"}, "tier_identity_applied": six keys each 1; "management_systems":{"operated_this_run":["dcms","bms","ems"],"document_control":{"ceo_directive":"9b1c278b1d24…","board_action_plan":"d57bf6cc77e6…","bto_programme":"568347fa4ddc…","build_to_order":"2358d91f3c2f…"}}
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding stands as assessed, reproduced with independent input, and I found no tier-1 hazard to correct it toward.  WHAT IS REAL. The appraise-and-develop loop genuinely closes. Six appraisal edges run every cascade (chief_appraises_board, board_appraises_ceo, ceo_appraises_csuite, csuite_appraises_coe, ceo_appraises_bto, bto_appraises_build); each one's "## Development Action" is extracted and persisted; the next cascade reads it and injects it into the appraised (lower) tier's prompt, and reports exactly which run_id set it. My two runs chained correctly (d53b0c6c3f → c0624a4e7d → 9b6fbf5e9a) and the persistent identity count incremented 13 → 14. Development Actions do have a consumer, and the direction is strictly downward, so §5's arms-length clause is not violated by the loop.  ONE OBSERVATION THAT DOES NOT CHANGE THE VERDICT. Under this environment (AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1) every tier is served by the deterministic native structured engine, and I verified that all six Development Actions in my run 2 are BYTE-IDENTICAL: "- Validate the structured outputs above against the stated objective. / - Route richer generation to a model resource (local-first; external only if enabled). / - Iterate via the orchestrator's cascade for the stated domain; record outcomes to memory/UEG." So the loop carries a CONSTANT rather than a tier-specific improvement, and I could find no trace of the injected DEVELOPMENT ACTION / TIER IDENTITY text in the tier outputs themselves. By the task's own rule this is the environment, not a defect — and critically, the surface SAYS so: every appraisal opens with "_[Workstation native structured engine — owned, no external dependency]_" and carries "The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them", and quality.qms_basis on my run reads "not assessable — floor-served: the deterministic floor emits the requested headings, so coverage cannot fail by construction and the stub regex never matches its vocabulary; no instrument here can say whether this content is good". Nothing certifies what it could not assess.  I also checked for an over-claiming consumer and found none: grep across all .ts/.tsx/.jsx/.js returns zero readers of development_applied or tier_identity_applied, so the only reached surface is the API JSON, whose own field semantics ("key → the run_id that set it", "edge → runs served so far") are exactly what I measured. No surface asserts the actions are substantive.  TWO SMALL CORRECTIONS TO THE ASSESSOR'S WORDING, neither material. (a) The extraction has an honest fallback (swarm.py:858-860): when the "## Development Action" header is absent the WHOLE appraisal is stored as the action, and I confirmed this already happened historically — data/tier_development.json's board_appraises_ceo row from run 31b6cc754e holds an action beginning "Appraisal of the AI CEO's Directive: Strengths:", i.e. an appraisal mislabelled to the next cycle's prompt as a Development Action. This is prompt-internal, disclosed in the code comment, and never user-facing. (b) The identity counter increments for all six edges unconditionally each cascade, with no guard that the appraised tier produced anything, so "you have served N cascade runs" counts cascades in which the edge was appraised rather than runs the tier demonstrably served; with every tier always running these are currently equal.  Tier set to 3 to record that this is a non-defect observation: nothing here is a reached surface stating an untruth, fabricating a figure, or certifying the unassessable, so it contributes nothing to M1's tier-1 count.

### R4.1 · The §8 homeostatic "posture" is decided by the caller's requested concurrency, so every linear swarm cascade reports a healthy organism as "protected" and the fabric console's posture badge can never read green — **PARTIAL** *(assessed DOC_OVERCLAIM)* · tier 2 *(assessed tier 1)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A reached badge tells the user the living organism is in a defensive posture on every cascade run, when the organism's own measured state is nominal and nothing throttled anything — the label encodes the caller's argument, not the organism. The same file already discloses one unreachable posture (`conserving_posture_reachable: false`) and says nothing about this second unreachable one.
- **claim:** §6 — "dynamic, adaptive, responsive, intelligent, autonomous workflow / pipeline / cascade trees"; §17.2 — "Cardiovascular — dynamic resource allocation (distribute)" + "homeostasis loops (immune↔nervous↔metabolic)"
- **observed:** Executed POST /api/v1/resources/swarm/run on the stored cascade swarm-f8fab215. It returned posture "protected" with max_parallel 1 while the same payload reported mode NOMINAL, composite_health 0.941, immune_threat NOMINAL and should_throttle false. Read agentic_core/ai/native/homeostasis.py:80 — `if bool(rec.get("should_throttle")) or cap == 1 or atp < _ATP_CONSERVE_AT: posture = "protected"` whose own comment is "organism is defending itself — minimal cognitive load" — and agentic_core/ai/native/orchestrator.py:893, which passes `requested_parallel=1` for every linear cascade, so `cap` is 1 unconditionally and "protected" is the only posture a cascade can ever report. Separately, GET /api/v1/native-ai/homeostasis calls assess(demand_nodes=0, requested_parallel=4) (homeostasis.py:165) while agentic_core/organism/biobus.py:405 caps the recommendation at `3 if _decider >= 0.7 else 1`, so `cap < requested_parallel` always holds and posture can never be "full". The surfaces render it: ResourceFabric.tsx:715 shows a "§8 {posture}" badge, and NativeAI.tsx:637-639 paints 'full' emerald, 'reduced' amber and anything else the alarm colour — the emerald branch is dead.
- **evidence:** Run sr-30a24987: {"posture":"in-house-first",…,"homeostasis":{"max_parallel":1,"posture":"protected","organism":{"mode":"NOMINAL","composite_health":0.941,"immune_threat":"NOMINAL","should_throttle":false}}}. homeostasis.py:80 `or cap == 1 or`; orchestrator.py:893 `homeo = homeostasis.assess(demand_nodes=len(stages), requested_parallel=1)`; biobus.py:405 `"max_parallel_agents": 3 if _decider >= 0.7 else 1`.
- **refutation: STOOD, verdict CORRECTED DOC_OVERCLAIM → PARTIAL.** The MECHANISM reproduces exactly, with my own input. In homeostasis.py:80 `if bool(rec.get("should_throttle")) or cap == 1 or atp < _ATP_CONSERVE_AT: posture = "protected"`, and orchestrator.py:893 passes requested_parallel=1 for every linear cascade, so cap = min(1, 3) = 1 and "protected" is the only posture a bespoke cascade can report — my sr-ceabd8d4 returned it against composite_health 1.0, immune NOMINAL, should_throttle false. The converse also reproduces: biobus.py:405 caps the recommendation at `3 if _decider >= 0.7 else 1` while homeostasis.py:165 snapshot() asks for 4, so `cap < requested_parallel` holds unconditionally and `posture == "full"` is unreachable on the rendered path — the live route returned "reduced" on a perfectly healthy organism, and NativeAI.tsx's emerald branch is dead (its only other route to "full" is the organism-unavailable fail-open, which returns organism: null and so does not render the card at all, NativeAI.tsx:630 `{homeo?.organism && ...}`).  But the TIER-1 claim is refuted, and it is refuted on the assessor's own cited surface. ResourceFabric.tsx:714-715 renders `runResult.org_cascade.homeostasis.posture`, which is fed by resource_fabric.py:1295 from the ORG cascade at swarm.py:391 — `requested_parallel=min(4, len(_sel_probe[:9]))` = 4, not 1. My independently created composition proved it: that badge printed "reduced", max_parallel 3. The assessor conflated two different assess() call sites. The "protected" value they observed exists ONLY in the JSON body of POST /api/v1/resources/swarm/run, and neither frontend consumer of that route even declares the field — NativeAI.tsx:18 `interface SwarmRun { agent; stages; trace; final; any_external }` and VSBSpawnStudio.tsx:85-88 `interface SwarmRunResult { stages; any_external; trace }` both omit `homeostasis`, and NativeAI's `Trace` component (line 53) renders only the per-stage trace. I grepped the whole superapp for the literal 'protected': zero hits. The other posture surfaces (Deliverables.tsx:270 via deliverables.py:265, which takes the default requested_parallel=4; ResourceFabric.tsx:536 via resource_fabric.py:1879 -> project() -> snapshot()) all take the 4-path and so also print "reduced", which I confirmed live on /compose/simulate.  So no reached surface prints "protected", no user is told the organism is defending itself, and no figure is fabricated. What a user does see is "§8 reduced" / "§8 capacity: reduced · admits 3 · fits" on a composite_health 1.0 NOMINAL organism. That label is weakly true rather than false: circadian genuinely was MAINTENANCE_REST (not ACTIVE_FOCUS), which independently selects "reduced" at homeostasis.py:81, and "admits 3" is the correct admitted cap. The real residue is a design defect plus a disclosure gap — the posture label is an artifact of the caller's requested_parallel for sequential callers, and the same block that explicitly discloses one unreachable posture (`conserving_posture_reachable: false`, with its basis rendered at NativeAI.tsx:650-655) says nothing about the second unreachable one ("full"), whose unreachability is a pure arithmetic consequence of 4 > 3. That is a tier-2 incomplete-disclosure finding about real, running machinery that does read live immune/self-healing/circadian state, not a tier-1 untruth. Verdict corrected DOC_OVERCLAIM/tier 1 -> PARTIAL/tier 2 (MILDER on tier; the surface sub-claim itself is refuted).  Two notes for the fix, since the assessor's prescription would miss: changing what the shared `posture` VALUE means would break four readers (ResourceFabric 536 and 715, Deliverables 270, NativeAI 637) — add a field (e.g. `posture_basis` / `full_posture_reachable`, mirroring the existing `conserving_posture_reachable` pattern) rather than redefining the string. And the honest fix for orchestrator.py:893 is to stop asking a concurrency question of a sequential cascade at all (its own comment already admits "the cascade runs sequentially, so concurrency isn't capped") — pass the real demand and let the posture come from organism terms, or omit the posture from that response.
- **refuter's evidence:** Ran my own ad-hoc 2-stage cascade (sr-ceabd8d4) -> posture "protected" on a NOMINAL, health-1.0 organism (mechanism reproduced); then created my own composition comp-6dc48d8c and ran it -> the badge the assessor cites (ResourceFabric.tsx:715, fed by swarm.py:391 with requested_parallel=4) printed "reduced", max_parallel 3, refuting the surface claim. Confirmed /native-ai/homeostasis and /compose/simulate both return "reduced" with cap 3, and grepped the superapp: no consumer of /resources/swarm/run declares or renders `homeostasis`, and the literal 'protected' appears on no surface.

### R4.2 · Every quality record states that the Endocrine layer's code is called by nothing, while the module §17.2 names as that layer is imported by 33 modules and is fired two lines above the statement — **DOC_OVERCLAIM** · tier 2 *(assessed tier 1)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A statement published on every delivery record, and rendered to a reader as a tooltip, is false twice over: it denies that a layer runs on a path where that layer just fired, and it credits a module with an integral term the same file measured as absent. A basis string is load-bearing code; this one measures the wrong module and contradicts its own file.
- **claim:** §17.2 — "7. Endocrine — signal bus / shared context + MJM (coordinate)"; realisation map: "Endocrine → the biomimetic signal bus + shared context (`organism/biobus.py`) — REAL as the bus"
- **observed:** Executed POST /api/v1/resources/compose/simulate; the returned §10 quality record carries `layer_states.Endocrine = "code_exists_unreached"` whose declared meaning (agentic_core/vbs/quality.py:36) is "a module exists and NOTHING IMPORTS IT, so the layer does not run anywhere". The table entry (quality.py:58-66) measures agentic_core/biomimicry/geospheric/regulator.py — not the signal bus §17.2 names. Grep for `organism.biobus` across agentic_core returns 33 importing modules, and quality.py:534 itself calls `biobus.fire_signal(...)` on the very code path that then publishes "unreached". The claim travels to users: apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/api.ts:286-295 (`layerTitle`) turns that state into the tooltip "code exists but nothing calls it: Respiratory, Endocrine", rendered on Deliverables.tsx:257 and components/organism/SwarmIntelligence.tsx:305. The same basis string also asserts the module "holds a real HomeostaticRegulator with an integral term", while quality.py:506-514 records the opposite measurement.
- **evidence:** Response field: "Endocrine":"code_exists_unreached" with layer_basis "agentic_core/biomimicry/geospheric/regulator.py holds a real HomeostaticRegulator with an integral term and NOTHING IMPORTS IT". Contradicted by quality.py:508-512: "`integral_error` is assigned once in __init__ as zeros and never accumulated, so there is no integral term … the corrective action returns a STRING, so nothing actuates". And quality.py:534: `biobus.fire_signal("cognitive", f"qms.{label}", …)`.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). HALF OF THE FINDING REPRODUCES EXACTLY, HALF IS A DOC-VS-CODE MAPPING DISPUTE.  Reproduced: the Endocrine basis string published on every quality record credits agentic_core/biomimicry/geospheric/regulator.py with "a real HomeostaticRegulator with an integral term", while that very file's own docstring (lines 13-14, 38) measures the opposite ("integral_error is assigned once below as a dict of zeros and never accumulated, so there is no integral term"; "It does not perform PID regulation (FU-307)"), and quality.py:506-514 records the same measurement 450 lines further down the same file. A published statement asserting a property its own named file measures as absent is a real truth defect, and I reproduced it on my own independently chosen composition. The executing guard (test_mvp_spine.py:25298-25311) checks only that the named module exists and has no importer - it never checks the basis's property claims, which is exactly how this clause survived 80 rounds.  REFUTED sub-claims, which is why the tier comes down: 1. "Measures the wrong module ... is fired two lines above the statement." The 33 importers and the fire_signal at quality.py:534 belong to agentic_core/organism/biobus.py. The CODE assigns that bus to the NERVOUS row (quality.py:44-46, state engaged_no_value, basis "the fire_signal below routes into the NervousSystem"), and biobus.py:2 self-describes as "the central nervous integration layer". The module the Endocrine row names, regulator.py:10, self-describes as "The 'Endocrine System' of the Workstation" and has ZERO importers (verified by grep). So "nothing imports it" is TRUE of the module measured; the assessor's argument requires preferring the vision doc's Endocrine->biobus mapping over the code's own, which is a defensible disagreement, not a false statement. 2. The travel-to-users chain is overstated. Deliverables.tsx:257 does not render layerTitle at this HEAD (it renders an immune-only tooltip); the real sites are SwarmIntelligence.tsx:305 and GenesisJourney.tsx:857. More decisively, NO frontend reads layer_basis - grep for "integral" across apps/workstation-superapp/src returns nothing - so the false integral-term sentence never reaches a rendered surface. What does reach a tooltip is only the state word, and that word is true of the module the table measures. 3. The verdict name is mislocated. The canon does not over-claim; it already DISCLOSES this exact defect: WHOLE_VISION.md:612 "its Endocrine basis over-claims the file it names (FU-307)" and :806 "the PID regulator file the W434 quality note vouched for is imported by nothing (the note is corrected in P2.7)". The defect lives in quality.py's LAYER_STATE data, not in a document. DOC_OVERCLAIM is the nearest available verdict for "a published statement asserts more than the measurement supports", so I keep it, but the location is the code's record payload.  TIER: the M1 tier-1 test is a reached surface telling a user something untrue, fabricating a figure, or certifying what it could not assess. The statement that reaches a rendered surface is true of what it measures; the statement that is false describes a dead module's internals, sits only in the API record, reaches no rendered UI, fabricates no figure about the user's delivery, and certifies nothing. It is an undisclosed misdescription inside a record - tier 2, not tier 1.

Read C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\biomimicry\geospheric\regulator.py:7-42 (its own docstring, lines 13-14 and 38: integral_error never accumulates so "there is no integral term"; "It does not perform PID regulation (FU-307)") and C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\vbs\quality.py:36, 55-66, 506-514, 534. ### R4.3 · The global shell shows a pulsing dot titled "Cardiovascular" on every page whose value is the host machine's spare CPU; the basis string is fetched and rendered nowhere — **PARTIAL** · tier 2 *(assessed tier 1)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The most-reached surface in the product presents a host-machine reading as an organism vital with a simulated heart rate, under an anatomical name the platform's own §17.2 record says corresponds to no subsystem. The correcting basis is already in the payload and in the hook, and is dropped at the last step — the badge-in-the-DOM class.
- **claim:** §17.2 — "4. Cardiovascular — dynamic resource allocation (distribute)"; realisation map: "the 'cardiovascular' biometric is CPU-idle relabelled … there is no circulatory subsystem"
- **observed:** Executed GET /api/v1/biometrics/status: `cardiovascular.resource_flow` 49.3 with `resource_flow_basis` "host CPU headroom (100 − cpu%) — spare capacity, not workload". Read apps/workstation-superapp/src/hooks/useWorkstationBiometrics.ts:93-94 — the hook threads `resource_flow_basis` and `peristaltic_delay_basis` into its state — then grepped both strings across src/: no component reads either. BiometricStatus.tsx:167 and :192 render the dot with `title="Cardiovascular"` and nothing else, and its pulse duration is set from the same CPU figure as a heart rate (cardioDuration, :68-72, "75 BPM"/"~60 BPM"/"stressed, rapid"). The widget ships in components/layout/Shell.tsx:296, i.e. on every page of the product. The source comments are honest about what the number is; the surface is not.
- **evidence:** API: "cardiovascular":{"resource_flow":49.3,"host_cpu_percent":50.7,"resource_flow_basis":"host CPU headroom (100 − cpu%) — spare capacity, not workload"}. Surface: `title="Cardiovascular"` (BiometricStatus.tsx:167, :192); hook carries `resource_flow_basis: r.cardiovascular?.resource_flow_basis` (useWorkstationBiometrics.ts:93) with zero readers.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding's mechanism reproduces in full and nothing in its premise is wrong: the correcting basis string is computed by the backend, carried into hook state at useWorkstationBiometrics.ts:93-94, and dropped at the last step with zero component readers — textbook "a badge in the DOM is not a label on the text" / dropped-provenance class, on the most-reached surface in the product. PARTIAL stands. I correct the TIER down from 1 to 2, because the tier-1 gate is a reached surface telling a user something untrue, FABRICATING A FIGURE, or certifying what it could not assess, and this surface renders no figure at all. Three facts I established that the assessor's tier argument does not survive: (1) The shell widget prints no number. Its complete exposed text is title="Cardiovascular" and aria-label="Mesh Idle". The 27.0 figure never reaches the user — it only drives a colour class and a CSS animation-duration on a 6px decorative dot. No figure is fabricated, and host_cpu_percent is a genuine live measurement (it moved 73.0 -> 73.1 between my two samples). (2) The "simulated heart rate" is a SOURCE COMMENT, not a surface claim. "75 BPM" appears only in the comment at :69. A user sees a dot pulsing, never a rate. I also found the mapping is non-monotonic — flow>80 gives 0.8s, flow 40-80 gives 1.0s, flow<40 gives 0.5s — so an idle host pulses FASTER than a busy one. It is not even presented as a coherent rate, which weakens rather than strengthens the "presents a heart rate" reading. (3) The genuinely untrue RENDERED text was already removed. The only word this widget prints is stateLabel(systemState), and deriveSystemState (:58-69) carries an explicit W489 note that this label used to be `resource_flow > 60` — i.e. CPU-derived, so an idle machine displayed "Work" — and is now derived from workload.platform_busy, which my API call confirms is the platform's own projects and channels with its own honest basis ("the platform's own running projects and open channels"). The CPU figure no longer drives any rendered word. Checking one surface the assessor missed: Introspection.tsx:107 is the one place the resource_flow NUMBER is actually printed, and it is labelled `Host CPU headroom` with a W489 comment explaining the correction. So the surface that renders the figure DOES disclose; the surface that discloses nothing renders no figure. What remains is real and should be fixed: an anatomical name ("Cardiovascular") on an indicator whose input is the host machine's spare CPU, under a name the platform's own 17.2 realisation map says corresponds to no subsystem, with the correcting basis already in hand and thrown away one step from the DOM. That is a disclosure shortfall on a reached surface — tier 2 — not a user being told a false fact or shown an invented reading. The fix is one line: put resource_flow_basis into the title attribute.
- **refuter's evidence:** Executed GET /api/v1/biometrics/status myself twice, getting my own values (resource_flow 27.0 then 26.9; host_cpu_percent 73.0 then 73.1 — a live, varying psutil reading, not a fixed figure), with resource_flow_basis "host CPU headroom (100 - cpu%) - spare capacity, not workload". Read apps/workstation-superapp/src/hooks/useWorkstationBiometrics.ts:24-25 (interface) and :93-94 (assignment), then grepped resource_flow_basis and peristaltic_delay_basis across the whole of src/: FOUR hits total, all four being those declarations and assignments. Zero readers — reproduced exactly. Read components/BiometricStatus.tsx: :167 and :192 render the dot with title="Cardiovascular" and nothing else; cardioDuration (:68-72) sets the pulse from the same CPU-derived figure with "75 BPM"/"~60 BPM"/"stressed, rapid" comments; confirmed via grep of every aria-label and title= in the file that the widget's ONLY exposed text is three tooltips plus aria-label={`Mesh ${label}`} — no number is rendered. Confirmed the mount at components/layout/Shell.tsx:296 (global chrome). Additionally read pages/cognitive/Introspection.tsx:107, which the assessor did not check.

### R4.4 · User design control over the VSB organisation structure does not exist: the officer roster is a module literal and the Cockpit's "organisational hierarchy" is a hardcoded seven-tier array — **API_ONLY** *(assessed STUB)* · tier 1 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Real machinery exists for half the clause (cascade and resource reconfiguration are genuinely user-editable) and nothing exists for the other half, and the surface does not say so: the hierarchy card reads as this entity's structure, is identical for every entity, does not match the entity's own cascade, and offers no control.
- **claim:** §6 — "Greater user reconfiguration and design control of the VSB organisation structure and digital resources"; §7 — "users reconfigure the VSB organisation structure and the digital resources / swarm cascades"
- **observed:** Executed GET /api/v1/swarm/agents → 16 agents, "total":16; read agentic_core/api/swarm.py:55 — `_AGENTS: dict[str, dict] = {` is a module-level literal, and swarm.py:387 only filters a caller's `csuite_roles` against it (`[r for r in (req.csuite_roles or _DEFAULT_CSUITE) if r in _AGENTS …]`). Scanned all 486 paths in the live OpenAPI for an org-structure route (org/csuite/ceo/coe/bto/structure): none creates, renames or removes an officer or a tier. The only structural editor is the swarm-cascade stage list (POST/PUT /api/v1/resources/swarm, surfaced by NativeAI.tsx and the EditCascade panel at VSBCockpit.tsx:623). Meanwhile VSBCockpit.tsx:606-613 heads a card "Organisational hierarchy (apex → operational delivery)" and maps a literal seven-tier array for every entity, while the probe entity's own stored cascade org is a different five: Chief, AI CEO, C-Suite, Centre of Excellence, Build-to-Order.
- **evidence:** swarm.py:55 `_AGENTS: dict[str, dict] = {`; VSBCockpit.tsx:608 `{['Chief of Board', 'Board of Directors', 'AI CEO', 'C-Suite', 'Centres of Excellence', 'Business Transformation Office', 'Build-to-Order'].map(...)}`; GET /api/v1/resources/swarm → "org":["Chief (the Owner's standing charter; no twin model is trained)","AI CEO","C-Suite","Centre of Excellence","Build-to-Order"].
- **refutation: STOOD, verdict CORRECTED STUB → API_ONLY.** The observations reproduce, but the assessor's headline premise and their tier are both wrong, in opposite directions.  PREMISE REFUTED (they were too harsh on the machinery). Their claim that of 486 paths "none creates, renames or removes an officer or a tier" is false for tiers. PUT /api/v1/resources/swarm/{sid} accepts `org` and rewrites the tier list; I renamed, removed and replaced VarroaGuard's five tiers with five of my own invention and read them back. That is genuine §6/§7 design control over the VSB organisation structure, working, in the live backend. So the title "does not exist" is wrong and STUB is the wrong label: this is API_ONLY. Officer-roster control genuinely is MISSING - _AGENTS is a module literal, no route adds an officer, and the only registry route takes a 5-value role enum and discloses in its own payload that it gates nothing. That half of the clause is correctly described.  TIER CORRECTED UP TO 1 (they were too kind on the surface). The assessor's own reasoning - "the hierarchy card reads as this entity's structure, is identical for every entity, does not match the entity's own cascade, and offers no control" - is a tier-1 description that they scored as tier 2. Once you know org IS user-editable, the card is worse than a missing feature: it is a reached surface contradicting the user's own saved configuration. After my PUT, the entity's stored structure was my five tiers while the Cockpit's "Organisational hierarchy (apex → operational delivery)" card for that same entity still printed its hardcoded seven. The user reconfigures the structure through the platform's own §7 route and the page headed with that structure shows the discarded default, with no qualifier, no tooltip and no platform-wide caveat.  It fails three ways at once, and none of them is the environment (AI_DISABLE_LOCAL is irrelevant here - no model is involved):   1. It contradicts the entity's own stored org. True even untouched: vsb-9e841ec4b1, which I never mutated, stores five tiers and the card shows seven.   2. It contradicts the platform's own authoritative hierarchy. GET /api/v1/board/status returns scope "workstation" and an eight-item hierarchy apexed on "Owner" and terminating in "Operational Delivery". The card omits the real apex (Owner) and stops at Build-to-Order, so its heading's promise of "apex → operational delivery" is unmet under the platform reading too. There is no reading of the card that is true.   3. The same user sees two different answers. VSBSpawnStudio.tsx:554 renders the entity's real org tiers; the Cockpit renders the literal.  Also worth flagging for whoever fixes this: VSBCockpit.tsx:615 is the "presence check cannot see a disabled gate" shape from memory - `{swarm.org && ...}` names org in the gate while the body never prints it, so any guard grepping VSBCockpit.tsx for org would go green over this exact defect. The fix is to render swarm.org in that card and extend EditCascade's PUT body to carry org, not to re-word the heading; the API already does the work.
- **refuter's evidence:** Read C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\api\swarm.py:55 (`_AGENTS: dict[str, dict] = {`, 16 officers) and executed GET /api/v1/swarm/agents -> total 16. Read apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/VSBCockpit.tsx:606-625 and confirmed the literal seven-tier array at :608 is mapped unconditionally.  Then I went past the assessor's grep. Their scan covered org/csuite/ceo/coe/bto/structure, so it missed the agent-registry and resource-fabric routes. GET /api/v1/swarm/agents still total 16, CRO absent. agent_hub.py:447 discloses it itself: "Registration gates nothing by itself - it is a presence record."   - GET /api/v1/organism/config -> org structure absent; most keys self-report "stored-only - nothing reads this key back".   - PUT /api/v1/resources/swarm/{sid} -> resource_fabric.py:1641 `if req.org is not None: c["org"] = req.org`, docstring ":1611 §7 user design control (W267) ... including a VSB's own delivery org: edit the name/stages/context/org tiers." ### R4.5 · The Genome layer governs nothing: the only adapter that would let a genome shape behaviour has zero callers and looks the store up by the wrong key — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The encode/mutate/crossover machinery is real and runs, but the layer's stated job — being the VSB's controlled identity, capabilities and constraints — is unimplemented, the bridge is dead code keyed to a different identifier, and the surface that invites a user to breed genomes says nothing about the result being inert.
- **claim:** §17.2 — "1. Genome — VSB identity/capabilities/constraints/evolution (arms-length controlled)"
- **observed:** Executed GET /api/v1/organism/status → `genome: {"total_genomes":0,"encoded_genomes":0,"mean_fitness":null,"dominant_trait_basis":"no genomes stored yet"}`. Read agentic_core/organism/biobus.py:518-545: `get_genome_context(entity_id)` and `genome_prompt_modifier(entity_id)` are the only code that would turn a genome into behaviour, and their docstrings claim consumers ("Used by CEO/C-Suite to adapt their reasoning", "Lets the CEO/swarm adapt behaviour to the entity's genetic profile"). Grepped both names repo-wide: the only hits are their own definitions and the one internal call — ZERO external callers. They also pass a VSB `entity_id` to `_load_genome`, which resolves a path from `genome_id` (agentic_core/organism/genome.py:63-66), so even if called it would always return None. Executed GET /api/v1/vsb/vsb-078e6b3af2/genome: a separate `genome_spec` dict with no trait vector, no capabilities and no constraints, `generation: 0`. The Anatomy page's Genome Lab (OrganismAnatomy.tsx:375-449) offers encode/mutate/crossover and never states that the result governs nothing.
- **evidence:** biobus.py:518 `def get_genome_context(self, entity_id: str)` → `_load_genome(entity_id)`; genome.py:63 `def _load_genome(genome_id: str) -> dict | None: p = _genome_path(genome_id)`; GET /api/v1/vsb/vsb-078e6b3af2/genome → {"epigenetic_traits":{"domain":"agriculture","origin":"genesis"},"generation":0}.
- **refutation: REFUTED — corrected to PARTIAL.** MILDER, and the tier must come down from 2 to 3.  The two dead biobus methods are real: zero external callers, and a signature promising entity_id over a store that only ever contains genome-*.json, so a VSB id resolves to None every time. I reproduced that and strengthened it by showing the function returns correct traits and a usable modifier when handed a genome_id — it is not broken machinery, it is working machinery wired to nothing.  But the title generalises from those two methods to the whole layer, and that inference is wrong. They are not "the only adapter". The VSB evolve route is a second, live, genome-reading path I executed myself, and it closes a loop — genome shapes the evolution prompt, proposals file to a CCA, Owner approval mutates epigenetic_traits and advances generation. That loop is precisely the vision clause's "evolution (arms-length controlled)". Add the enforced review_gates (ten call sites) and the genome_spec materialised into IDENTITY.md/genome.json, and §17.2's identity, constraints and evolution elements are substantially present. "Governs nothing" is refuted by the route I ran.  Tier 3, not 2, because no reached surface tells a user anything untrue. The Genome Lab header claims only "Encode an entity into a 10-axis trait genome, then mutate or cross lineages" and volunteers more than it has to — "no fitness here is ever evaluated (there is no selection step; the labels say inherited, not earned)". It never claims the result governs anything, so the assessor's charge is an omission, not a false statement, and omission is not the tier-1/2 bar. My own encode came back with its shortfall printed: the encoding_note renders at OrganismAnatomy.tsx:402, with a "NOT ENCODED" amber chip and grey defaulted trait bars. organism/status says total_genomes 0 with basis "no genomes stored yet". The evolve route states its own limits unprompted — generation_basis "a filed cycle does not advance it", outcome_basis "the genome mutates only on POST .../evolution/apply after the Owner approves". Every figure carries provenance; nothing is fabricated; nothing certifies what it could not assess. The untrue sentences are all in docstrings (biobus.py:521 and :535 naming CEO/C-Suite consumers that do not exist; genome.py:5-9 claiming traits "influence how the AI generates content, selects agents, and prioritises tasks"), and a docstring is not a surface a user reaches.  PARTIAL survives as the label, but for a narrower and almost opposite reason than the assessor gave. What is genuinely unimplemented is the 10-axis trait vector and §17.2's "capabilities". The vector lives entirely inside organism/genome.py; its only two external importers are the dead biobus bridge and resource_fabric.py:650, which calls encode_genome as a writer and reports a dominant axis but never feeds traits back into behaviour. So the organism's trait-vector genome and the VSB's genome_spec are two disjoint systems that never meet, and the trait axes shape nothing anywhere. That, plus dead code carrying an over-claiming docstring, is the honest finding — a documentation over-claim and an architectural gap, disclosed at every user surface.  One incidental defect outside this finding's scope: /genome-explorer is offered in CommandPalette.tsx:67 and SearchMeshModal.tsx:38 but no route for it is registered anywhere under apps/workstation-superapp/src — a dead nav entry.
- **refuter's evidence:** I reproduced every mechanical observation but refuted both load-bearing claims in the title.  REPRODUCED (my own inputs, not the assessor's): 1. Zero callers. Grep repo-wide for get_genome_context / genome_prompt_modifier: exactly 3 hits — biobus.py:518 and :532 (the two defs) and :537 (the internal call). No external caller. SAME. 2. The wrong key — and I proved it HARDER than the assessor, by showing the function works on the right key. With my own picks: biobus.get_genome_context("genome-0ccafcba74") returned the full 10-axis dict and genome_prompt_modifier returned a real live string, "Entity genome traits: prioritise revenue and commercial viability; emphasise long-term sustainability over short-term gain. ". The same calls with a VSB id — mine (vsb-d42be93723) and the assessor's (vsb-078e6b3af2) — both returned {}. glob("data/genomes/vsb-*.json") is empty, because _save_genome names the file from genome["genome_id"] = f"genome-{uuid}". So the bridge is functional but keyed to a namespace no VSB id can ever enter. SAME. 3. Encoded my own genome: POST /api/v1/organism/genome/encode {"entity_name":"Refuter R45 Beehive Telemetry","domain":"agriculture"} -> genome-6be1e945a5, all ten axes 0.5, encoded:false, fitness_provenance "default-unencoded (no FITNESS line parsed)", trait_provenance.parsed [] / defaulted all 10, and an encoding_note ending "this vector is NOT an analysis of the entity". 4. Read two VSB genomes I chose myself — vsb-d42be93723 (Nakhla Cold Chain Trust) and vsb-9e841ec4b1 (Neighbourhood Tool Library). Both: genome_spec of problem/domain/realm/concept/design/commercialisation/constitutional_gate, epigenetic_traits {domain, origin}, generation 0. No trait vector, no capabilities. SAME.  REFUTED — the premise: 5. "the only adapter" is false. POST /api/v1/vsb/{id}/evolve (vsb.py:2194-2214) reads genome_spec.concept + epigenetic_traits + applied_mutations and injects "Genome expression (evolve WITH the grain of this genome)" into the evolution prompt. I EXECUTED it on vsb-d42be93723: evolution_cycles_run 0->1, one proposal filed as cca-2fb68ca1c0, body repo re-shipped at commit ef7db379738d. 6. "governs nothing" is false. Three live paths: (a) that evolve cycle feeds a CCA whose apply writes proposals back into epigenetic_traits, appends applied_mutations and advances generation only when a mutation lands (vsb.py:2458-2476) — behind Owner CCA approval, which IS §17.2's "arms-length controlled"; (b) review_gates, documented at vsb.py:1365 as "set in the VSB genome", are enforced by _refuse_gated at ten movers (repo, website, web app, phone app, board pack, ship, repo cascade, evolve, evolution apply); (c) genome_spec is materialised into the body repo as IDENTITY.md and genome.json (vsb.py:133, 195).  Could not complete: POST /api/v1/cca/cca-2fb68ca1c0/review was denied by the permission classifier, so I verified the apply leg by code read plus the route's own outcome_basis rather than by executing it. cca-2fb68ca1c0 is left pending and no generation advanced.  Live-backend state I created (no repository file written): genome-6be1e945a5; one evolve cycle on vsb-d42be93723.

### R4.6 · The Respiratory layer has no implementation on any running path — the module the product names for it has no importer, and the Agent Hub the vision names instead subscribes no executor — **DOC_OVERCLAIM** *(assessed MISSING)* · tier 1 *(assessed tier 3)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The capability is absent rather than misreported: the hub route discloses that registration gates nothing, and the quality record discloses the layer as unreached, so no surface claims the layer acts.
- **claim:** §17.2 — "5. Respiratory — autonomous workflow cascade / Agent Hub (do)"; realisation map: "real bus, records only, no executor subscribed"
- **observed:** Grepped `triad_integration` / `TriadIntegrator` across the repo excluding _archive: the only hits are the class definition in agentic_core/molecular/triad_integration.py:13, the basis string in agentic_core/vbs/quality.py:62 that names it, a comment at quality.py:517, and one test reference — no importer. Executed GET /api/v1/hub/agents: `registered: []` with a 16-entry static platform roster, and the route states its own limit honestly.
- **evidence:** GET /api/v1/hub/agents → {"registered":[],"platform_roster":[…16…],"note":"`registered` lists external agent sessions that called /hub/agents/register; `platform_roster` is the swarm's live agent cast. Registration gates nothing by itself — it is a presence record."}; quality.py:62 basis "agentic_core/molecular/triad_integration.py holds a real TriadIntegrator and NOTHING IMPORTS IT".
- **refutation: STOOD, verdict CORRECTED MISSING → DOC_OVERCLAIM.** BOTH of the assessor's observations reproduce — but their tier is wrong by two steps, and one clause of their title is too strong.  WHAT REPRODUCES (SAME). No importer of triad_integration outside _archive (only the class def at :13, the quality.py basis at :62, the comment at :517/:545, and one test). And the hub subscribes no executor: my own work-order sat at `recorded` with delivered_to_live_subscribers: 0, and three reached surfaces say so in words — the POST response note, the GET /hub/handoffs note, and HandoffRequest's own docstring (agentic_core/api/agent_hub.py:169-175) which records that the old "Claude Code should execute these" claim was false.  WHY THE TITLE IS TOO STRONG (the MILDER half). "No implementation on any running path" is not what I measured. The vision's own realisation map (docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:804) names Respiratory as "the Agent Hub (W443) + the org cascade", and both run: the hub's registry, SSE bus and letterbox all worked on my input, and my own POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade executed an eight-tier autonomous workflow cascade in 5.6s on 16 native-served calls and persisted the run. What is absent is the EXECUTOR, not the layer.  WHY THE TIER IS 1, NOT 3 (the decisive correction). The assessor's tier rests on "no surface claims the layer acts". But the surface does not merely decline to claim — it ASSERTS something untrue, and I reached it myself. My cascade response carries biomimetic.layer_basis.Respiratory verbatim: "agentic_core/molecular/triad_integration.py holds a real TriadIntegrator and NOTHING IMPORTS IT", and biomimetic.layers_note: "CODE EXISTS BUT NOTHING CALLS IT — so the layer does not run anywhere: Respiratory, Endocrine". The file holds no real TriadIntegrator: five of the six molecular modules it imports were moved to _archive/jules-unwired/, so the module raises ModuleNotFoundError on import and the class can never be instantiated. The whole purpose of that sentence is to separate "real code, one wiring away" from "a stub" — quality.py:545 says "A file is not an implementation if nothing calls it" — and in this direction the distinction is false: this is not real-but-unwired, it is broken-and-unwireable. Nothing imports it because nothing CAN. A reader of any quality record, and anyone on the Living Organisation tooltip (apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/api.ts:287-295 renders "code exists but nothing calls it: Respiratory"), is told the layer is a wiring away from working. That is a reached surface stating an untruth about the platform's own state — the tier-1 bar, and not an AI_DISABLE_LOCAL artifact (no model is involved in a hardcoded basis string).  WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT. The guard written to keep this claim falsifiable checks the two properties that an unimportable file satisfies perfectly: the named module EXISTS (:25299) and has NO importer (:25303). It never imports it. The allowed state set {engaged_with_value, engaged_no_value, implemented_not_on_this_path, measurement_under_this_name, code_exists_unreached} has no state meaning "present but unimportable", so the honest answer is unexpressible — the same shape as my note "a basis string is code": the word "real" is unchecked prose sitting inside a field whose other properties are computed.  ADJACENT, FLAGGED NOT CLAIMED (out of R4.6's scope, likely another R4 row): the SAME live block's Endocrine basis asserts "a real HomeostaticRegulator with an integral term", while that module's own docstring (agentic_core/biomimicry/geospheric/regulator.py:13) states "`integral_error` is assigned once below as a dict of zeros and never accumulated, so there is no integral term" — and quality.py:505-515 records that FU-307 corrected exactly this wording. One file contradicts itself, and the surface ships the uncorrected half.  So: the capability is MISSING as assessed and that half is honestly disclosed (tier 3 on its own merits); what lifts the row is that the disclosure itself overclaims the module it points at. DOC_OVERCLAIM, tier 1, HARSHER. The fix is not to wire the triad — it is for the basis to say what is true ("the file is present but unimportable: five of its six molecular dependencies are archived") and for the guard to gain an import leg.
- **refuter's evidence:** All with my own inputs, not the assessor's. (1) HUB, my own agent: POST /api/v1/hub/agents/register {"agent_id":"refuter-r46-probe","role":"ENGINEERING","capability_tags":["respiratory-layer-probe"]} -> 201; GET /api/v1/hub/agents then showed MY record in `registered` (1 entry, roster 16) — so the registry is real, the assessor's `registered: []` was an empty room, not a broken store. I then filed my own work-order: POST /api/v1/hub/claude-code-handoff (from refuter-r46-probe, "R4.6 probe: does any executor pick this up") -> {"status":"recorded","delivered_to_live_subscribers":0,"note":"... No executor is subscribed to the hub — nothing will run this automatically."}; GET /api/v1/hub/handoffs still shows it at status `recorded` with note "records only — no executor is subscribed". Deregistered the probe afterwards (registered back to 0). (2) ORG CASCADE, my own mission: POST /api/v1/swarm/cascade {"mission":"Refuter R4.6 independent probe: stand up a tidal-flow irrigation co-op in the Humber estuary","csuite_roles":["CTO","COO"]} -> HTTP 200 in 5.6s, 53 KB, run_id fcadccd874, 8 tiers (chief/board/CEO/C-Suite/CoE/BTO/BTO-build/catalogue), ai_provenance {"served_by":{"native":16},"any_external":false}, persistence {"org_cascade_runs":{"persisted":true}}. (3) THE DECISIVE CHECK the assessor did not run — I tried to IMPORT the module the basis string certifies: `from agentic_core.molecular.triad_integration import TriadIntegrator` -> ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'agentic_core.molecular.p53_oscillator'. Inventory of its six imports: atp_simulator OK, and p53_oscillator / ubiquitin_system / hsp_network / redox_sensor / chaperone_cascade all MISSING (`ls agentic_core/molecular/` = __init__.py, atp_simulator.py, triad_integration.py, work_budget.py; `find` locates p53_oscillator.py only at _archive/jules-unwired/agentic_core/molecular/p53_oscillator.py). (4) Reproduced the guard's legs in-process: for Respiratory, `(root/mod).exists()` is True (the leg integration_tests/test_mvp_spine.py:25298 asserts) while importlib.import_module of the same dotted path raises — no leg of test_w506_p27_layers_state_per_layer_and_the_reachability_is_checked asserts importability, so the guard stays green over an unimportable file.

### R4.7 · No swarm is bespoke to a founder — the digital-twin model estate is empty, and the twin that §6 makes the basis of a per-founder cascade does not exist — **PARTIAL** *(assessed MISSING)* · tier 3

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The gap is disclosed in the same payload that would otherwise imply a founder twin, and the floor environment cannot train one anyway; the per-solution half of the clause is genuinely delivered.
- **claim:** §6 — "Bespoke-per-solution swarms … and bespoke to each founder (modelled as the founder's digital twin, specific to their instructions and data)"
- **observed:** Executed GET /api/v1/twin/models → {"models":[],"total":0}. Executed GET /api/v1/resources/swarm: the one stored cascade's `org` array names the apex as "Chief (the Owner's standing charter; no twin model is trained)" and its concept field reads "content pending the owned model — this enterprise has not yet composed its own concept", so the cascade is bespoke to the SOLUTION (its stages, context and VSB binding are per-entity) but not to a founder.

Two corrections, both MILDER.  (a) The stated evidence is wrong. The digital-twin model estate is not empty — it held 2 records before I ran anything, written by transformation_orchestration._generate_vsb_twin.
