The gap is narrower than "nothing exists": records exist, they are structural templates of the VSB delegation chain, explicitly stamped trained:False, and none of them is a founder twin. Nothing is overclaimed by their presence, so this correction does not create a new defect — but a finding whose evidence line is false as read must not stand on that line.  (b) MISSING overstates it. The clause has two conjuncts and the assessor concedes the first: swarms ARE bespoke per solution, and I verified that with my own input — my cascade is owner-stamped, VSB-bound, and its context carries my mission, my domain and my concept, not a template's. One conjunct delivered and one absent is PARTIAL, not MISSING.  Tier stays 3, and I probed specifically for a tier-1 escalation and did not find one. Every surface I reached for my own entity — the cascade org list, board.chief.title, the business-plan strategy sentence, /api/v1/board/status, the living-plan pillar — states "no twin model is trained" in the same payload that mentions the Chief. No reached surface claims a founder twin, fabricates a twin figure, or certifies twin fidelity it could not assess. The one residual hazard is that "no twin model is trained" is a static literal rather than a computed reading of the estate, so it would go stale the day a twin IS trained (the basis-string-is-code class) — but today it is true, since the only three records are untrained spec documents, so that is a latent robustness issue and not a false statement now.

### R4.8 · DELIVERED — bespoke, reconfigurable, re-runnable swarm cascades as first-class fabric resources, grounded in a live entity and ledger-audited — **DELIVERED**

- **claim:** §6 — "Reconfigurable by users. Greater user reconfiguration and design control … of the AI Agent Swarm cascades, workflows and pipelines" and "robust, reliable, resilient, reconfigurable, reusable"
- **observed:** Executed GET /api/v1/resources/swarm (one stored cascade with user-authored stages, usage_area, posture, reusable/rerunnable flags and a vsb_id) then POST /api/v1/resources/swarm/run with swarm_id swarm-f8fab215. All four stages ran in sequence (ai-ceo → c-suite → centre-of-excellence → build-to-order, 1002/1439/1449/1433 chars of output), every step reported the resource that served it, and the run was written to the UEG hash chain. POST /swarm/define, PUT/DELETE /resources/swarm/{sid} exist in the live OpenAPI, and VSBCockpit.tsx:623 mounts an EditCascade whose saved stages the next run re-reads.
- **evidence:** {"run_id":"sr-30a24987","swarm_id":"swarm-f8fab215","vsb_id":"vsb-078e6b3af2","grounded_in_live_vsb":true,"served_by_all":["native"],"requests_not_honoured":[],"stages":4,"any_external":false,"audit":{"logged":true,"ueg_hash":"ba1400d3cdde0196…"}}
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The finding stands as DELIVERED, SAME direction. Every element of the vision clause is reachable machinery on my own input: bespoke (I authored arbitrary roles and instructions in an arbitrary order and they ran in that order), reconfigurable (PUT changed the stage count and role, and the next run re-read it with no apply step), re-runnable (two runs of one cascade, three of the family), first-class fabric resource (full CRUD with 404 after delete), grounded in a live entity (my VSB's own mission text appears in the run's stage context, and a non-existent entity is refused 404 rather than fabricated), and ledger-audited (my three operations are consecutive nodes in a chain that independently recomputes to valid:true).  No tier-1 condition is present, and I looked for one specifically. The stage outputs are not reasoning - they are the native structured engine's term counts, which is the AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1 environment. The decisive question is whether the reached surface says so, and it does, in band, inside the output text a user reads: "_[Workstation native structured engine - owned, no external dependency]_", "Extracted by counting words and adjacent pairs - not an analysis of the subject.", "framing the request above rather than analysing it", "The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them", and "Route richer generation to a model resource (local-first; external only if enabled)". Nothing on the surface claims specialist deliverables, analysis or intelligence. The same discipline holds for the adjacent fields I checked on the same response: homeostasis.atp_measured is false with a basis string saying the metabolic term is a simulator, and the VSB spawn stream labels its own cognitive cascade "Six Cognitive Engines (fixed responses)" with engines_computed:false and basis "each engine returns a literal; nothing here was derived from the challenge". No figure is fabricated and nothing certifies what it could not assess.  Two real but non-tier-1 shortfalls, neither of which touches R4.8's claim: (a) COVERAGE GAP worth a follow-up, tier 3: a VSB spawned through POST /api/v1/vsb/spawn gets native_swarm = null (I confirmed this on my own vsb-d3e5be555a), because the cascade is attached only on the genesis/establish path (agentic_core/api/genesis.py:802-819 calls rf.register_swarm and sets entity["native_swarm"]). So for a spawn-path entity the Cockpit's cascade line and the Edit cascade control never render. This is an absence, not an untruth - swarm.org is falsy so no swarm is claimed - and the swarm resource itself is still fully reachable via /resources/swarm/define with an explicit vsb_id, which is how I grounded mine. (b) QUALITY, tier 3: because the native engine counts terms, stage N+1 frames over stage N's own disclaimer boilerplate ("external dependency", "structured engine", "owned external" appeared as the "most frequent terms" of my step 2). Prior output IS passed forward, so the chaining is real, but under the disabled-model environment the chaining carries no substance. The engine labels its output as a term count rather than an analysis, so this is a weakness of the fallback, not a false statement. Also noted and not a defect: owner_id recorded as "default" despite my X-Owner-Id: refuter_r48 header - single-user mode, and no surface claims otherwise.
- **refuter's evidence:** I did not re-run the assessor's swarm-f8fab215. I built my own and drove the whole lifecycle.  1. RE-RUN re-read the edit with nothing further to apply: run_id sr-fe2371f1, name "Masjid Night-Shelter (R4.8 EDITED)", stages:1, role legal-compliance. This is the load-bearing half of the reconfigurability claim and it held. 5. LEDGER, independently verified not just reported: GET /api/v1/gaas/ueg/verify -> valid:true, 579 events, anchor_state "present", basis "every stored hash recomputed, and the chain head matches the tail anchor". GET /api/v1/gaas/ueg/events then showed MY three operations as consecutive chained nodes event_524 (swarm.run sr-9d7f757d), event_525 (swarm.reconfigured, stages 1), event_526 (swarm.run sr-fe2371f1), each previous_hash matching the prior node's hash and event_525.previous_hash equal to the ueg_hash the run response had returned to me. 6. Defined swarm-770eaf9b against it, ran it -> vsb_id vsb-d3e5be555a, grounded_in_live_vsb:true, and the stage context carried MY entity's real mission text ("Allotment growers in Leicester cannot store winter root crops...") pulled at run time, so the grounding is substantive and not a flag. 7. PUT with a blank role/instruction -> 400 "A cascade needs at least one stage, each with a role and an instruction", and the stored cascade was left intact. PUT with stages:[] -> same 400. DELETE /resources/swarm/swarm-aec6bf96 -> {"deleted":...,"remaining":7}, then GET -> 404 and RUN -> 404. 8. CODE: agentic_core/api/resource_fabric.py:1800-1814 - the ledger result is honest by construction, ledger defaults to {"logged": false, "why": "not attempted"} and an exception is reported as {"logged": false, "why": "<class>: <msg>"} rather than swallowed into a claim it was logged. apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/VSBCockpit.tsx:622 - EditCascade is gated on {swarm.cascade_id && ...}, a real data condition, not {false && ...}; swarm comes from detail?.native_swarm || {} at line 453, and the component (lines 46-120) does a real GET then a real PUT and disables Save on a blank stage, matching the server's 400 I reproduced.

TIER CORRECTED DOWN TO 3 (the assessor stated no tier). There is no tier-1 content here: every facility ran on the deterministic floor because AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1, which is the ENVIRONMENT, and the surfaces say so — run-level quality_warning "The gate could not assess this design's pre-run simulation (floor/template-served): commit at your judgement — nothing here certifies it", any_external false, each output text headed "_[Workstation native structured engine — owned, no external dependency]_", spi's floor_calls 8 of 8, and lib/api.ts:69-110 mapping served_by 'native'/'template' to the amber "structured floor — not model analysis" badge with the explicit third state "provenance not recorded" for an absent record. Nothing fabricated a figure and nothing certified what it could not assess.  THREE SMALL RIDERS I REPRODUCED, none of which overturns the verdict: (a) The assessor's "0 missing endpoints" is true for PATHS but their check did not cover METHODS. Two catalogue rows advertise methods ["POST"] against GET-only routes: POST /api/v1/organism/nervous/status → 405 and POST /api/v1/organism/genome → 405 (GET → 200). Tier 3, and neither is one of the eight §7 digital resources. An absent badge, not a false one; the floor disclosure still reaches the user via the run-level quality_warning and the output's own header. Tier 3. ## R5 — §1–§3, §3A, §9, §14, §15, §17.1 — the offerings, the avatar/UX, democratisation, the founding principles, the 4×6×4 grid

### R5.0 · Both global navigation surfaces advertise eight named capabilities that resolve to "Page Not Found" — **STUB** · tier 1

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** These are reached surfaces — one is on the home page, the other on every page via Ctrl+K — and each row names a capability with a description ("Immersive spatial computing lab", "Sovereign file management system", "Gene regulatory network dashboard") that the product does not have at all. The user is told the capability exists, selects it, and lands on 404. §15.10's resolution for exactly this case is retirement, and the archive comment in App.tsx:254 shows the pages WERE archived — the navigator that pointed at them was not.
- **claim:** §15.10 — "anything **not wired to vision realisation is archived** (never deleted — history-preserving); stubs and mocks never ship"; §9 "**guidance/navigation** through any platform area"; recorded §9 ruling: "**only wired, functional surfaces are routed** (aspirational pages archived, never shown)"
- **observed:** Read apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/SearchMeshModal.tsx (the modal the home page opens from its "Search Mesh" button, DashboardNew.tsx:170) and apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/layout/CommandPalette.tsx (mounted in Shell.tsx:172, opened by Ctrl/Cmd+K on EVERY page, CommandPalette.tsx:11-14). Extracted all 26 `route:` values from the search index and diffed them against all 76 `<Route path=` values in App.tsx: eight indexed destinations are not routed — /ar-vr, /fed-map, /file-hub, /forge, /genome-explorer, /grn-dashboard, /introspection, /orchestrator. App.tsx:257 is the catch-all `<Route path="*">` rendering "Page Not Found". The Command Palette independently offers three of the same dead routes (CommandPalette.tsx:52 "Federation Portal" → /fed-map, :66 "Introspection" → /introspection, :67 "Genome Explorer" → /genome-explorer). The same diff the other way: 56 of the 76 routed surfaces are absent from the index — including /genesis, /domains, /my-work, /qep, /vsb, /economy, /ai-tools — so the modal returns its "No results for …" branch (SearchMeshModal.tsx:146) for the platform's own flagship journey.
- **evidence:** SearchMeshModal.tsx:48 `{ id: 'ar-vr', label: 'AR/VR Lab', description: 'Immersive spatial computing lab', category: 'Advanced', icon: Camera, route: '/ar-vr' }`; :49 `label: 'Federation', description: 'Multi-node federation map', route: '/fed-map'`; :50 `label: 'File Hub', description: 'Sovereign file management system', route: '/file-hub'`; :39 `label: 'GRN Mesh', description: 'Gene regulatory network dashboard', route: '/grn-dashboard'`. App.tsx:257-260 `<Route path="*" element={ … <h2 …>Page Not Found</h2>`.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). STANDS at STUB / tier 1, direction SAME.  The tier-1 test is a reached surface telling a user something untrue. Both conditions hold and I verified each myself. Reached: the modal is one click from the home page header, and the palette is Ctrl+K on every page in both the mobile and desktop Shell branches. Untrue: the modal renders ALL 26 entries with an empty query (filteredFlat falls through to SEARCH_INDEX at :92) and its footer prints "26 results", so a user who opens it and types nothing is shown eight capability rows — each with a confident product description — that resolve to "Page Not Found". This is not the AI_DISABLE_LOCAL environment and nothing about it is model-dependent; it is a static routing table. Nothing on either surface discloses it: no disabled state, no "coming soon", no archived marker, and no follow-up row anywhere in docs/FOLLOWUPS.json. §15.10's own remedy was in fact applied to the pages — App.tsx:253-254 carries the comment that the AR/VR and related experimental pages were "archived to _archive/frontend-pages", and I confirmed ARVRSandbox.tsx and IntrospectionDashboard.tsx are sitting in that directory — but the two navigators that point at them were left advertising them. That is precisely the §9 ruling's failure mode ("only wired, functional surfaces are routed"), inverted: the surface was unrouted and the navigator was not.  One leg of the assessor's rationale I correct without softening the tier. They wrote that each row "names a capability that the product does not have at all." That is true for five — /ar-vr, /fed-map, /file-hub, /genome-explorer and /grn-dashboard have no routed equivalent anywhere in App.tsx. It is wrong for three: /forge has a real /forge-pipeline (App.tsx:205), /introspection has a real /cognitive-introspection (App.tsx:249), and "Homeostasis" has /organism plus the /heartbeat redirect (App.tsx:165-166). Those three are broken links to live machinery rather than advertisements for vapour. That distinction changes the FIX (three are a one-word path correction, five are retirements) but not the tier, because the user-visible outcome is identical: select the row, land on "Page Not Found". A navigator row is itself a claim that its destination exists, and for all eight that claim is false at the surface.  The second half of the finding is independently as damaging and I reproduced it with inputs the assessor never tried: the index omits 57 of the 75 real routes, so the platform's flagship journeys are unfindable by name. "genesis", "vsb", "economy", "my work", "qep" and "ai tools" each return the "No results for" branch. A user searching the Sovereign Mesh for the Sovereign Mesh's own headline products is told, in effect, that they do not exist — while the eight that do not exist are offered unprompted. That inversion is the §9 guidance clause failing in both directions at once from a single surface.  Not milder, not harsher: the count is 8, the palette overlap is 3, the surfaces are reached on every page, and no disclosure exists. Tier 1 confirmed, which means this one counts against the M1 target of zero.
- **refuter's evidence:** I did not repeat the assessor's diff — I wrote my own parser. At HEAD e0d13ab1 I extracted the 26 SEARCH_INDEX entries from apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/SearchMeshModal.tsx (label/description/category/route tuples, via regex over the literal, not the `route:` strings alone) and the 76 `<Route path=` values from apps/workstation-superapp/src/App.tsx, then set-differenced them in node. Result: exactly 8 dead destinations — /genome-explorer (Genome, "System evolution & GRN mesh"), /grn-dashboard (GRN Mesh, "Gene regulatory network dashboard"), /introspection (Introspection, "Cognitive self-analysis & memory"), /orchestrator (Homeostasis, "Organism balance & vital signs"), /forge (The Forge, "Developer tooling & pipelines"), /ar-vr (AR/VR Lab, "Immersive spatial computing lab"), /fed-map (Federation, "Multi-node federation map"), /file-hub (File Hub, "Sovereign file management system"). The same 8 as assessed.  I then ran twelve search strings I chose myself through the component's actual filter predicate (SearchMeshModal.tsx:86-92, label||description||category substring, lowercased). Five of mine land on a dead row: "immersive" -> /ar-vr, "sovereign file" -> /file-hub, "gene regulatory" -> /grn-dashboard, "homeostasis" -> /orchestrator, "forge" -> /forge. Six of mine hit the "No results for" branch (SearchMeshModal.tsx:146) although the route exists and is wired: "genesis", "vsb", "economy", "my work", "qep", "ai tools" — typing "qep" in the Sovereign Mesh search finds nothing even though /qep, /qep-religion and the Religion hub are all routed. My reverse diff is 57 routed-but-unindexed of the 75 non-catch-all routes (the assessor said 56 of 76 — same fact, off by whether `*` is counted).  Reachability I confirmed independently, not from their line numbers: DashboardNew.tsx:149 mounts the modal and :166 is the Button that opens it on the home page header. Shell.tsx mounts CommandPalette TWICE — :172 in the isMobile branch and :212 in the desktop branch — so Ctrl/Cmd+K (CommandPalette.tsx:11-14) opens it on every page on both layouts. Both navigators call react-router navigate() directly (SearchMeshModal.tsx:99-103 `go`; CommandPalette.tsx:21-25 `go`), and App.tsx:257-264 is the only `<Route path="*">`, rendering `<h2 ...>Page Not Found</h2>`. I grepped for a second route registry: App.tsx is the ONLY file in the app containing `<Route `, and there is no Navigate alias for any of the 8 (App.tsx has 24 Navigate redirects; none covers them). I independently re-derived the Command Palette's own list: 14 Items, 3 dead — :52 Federation Portal -> /fed-map, :66 Introspection -> /introspection, :67 Genome Explorer -> /genome-explorer. Exactly as assessed.  Also found, which the assessor did not: apps/workstation-superapp/src/hooks/useAvatarSession.ts:76,79,80 carries avatar-context prefixes for /forge, /orchestrator and /introspection — a third consumer of the dead paths. And Sidebar.tsx contains none of the 8, so the sidebar is clean; the rot is confined to the two search surfaces.  No frontend dev server was listening (5173/5174/3000/4173 all refused; backend :8086 /health = 200), so a click-through was not available — but routing here is client-side React Router resolved entirely from App.tsx, which I read in full, so the static resolution IS the behaviour. I also checked for an existing guard or disclosure: no test, script or doc references SEARCH_INDEX / SearchMeshModal / CommandPalette outside five docs/knowledge/*.json forensic dumps, and docs/FOLLOWUPS.json has no row for a dead nav route or a 404.

### R5.1 · The Employment Application Studio renders the prompt's own JSON placeholder as a job listing and reports "Synthesised 1 illustrative listing" — **STUB** · tier 1

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A reached surface states a figure ("Synthesised 1 illustrative listing") that is false — nothing was synthesised; the row is the request template reflected — and it offers an action on that non-listing. The amber floor badge beside it says which engine served, not that the content is the prompt's own scaffold, so the one honest sentence the page owns here never prints.
- **claim:** §3A offering 1 — "the six domains carry tool sets of comparable scope … (the Employment parity directive, incl. the application-form / supporting-statement tool that never invents experience the candidate lacks)"; §15.6 "**honesty over polish** (never fabricate; label simulation)"
- **observed:** Executed `POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/career/job-search` with `{"role":"Data Scientist","location":"London"}`. The deterministic floor echoes the prompt's field-template line back; agentic_core/api/career.py:295-296 defines that template and :303-320 parses any `{…}` line into a listing, so the echo becomes a row with `total: 1`. apps/workstation-superapp/src/components/employment/ApplicationStudio.tsx:450-452 then prints the count sentence, :468-500 renders the card (title, company, location, "est. {salary_estimate}", description) and :491-497 offers a "Use as Target Job Ad" button that posts to /api/v1/career/job-search/use and ingests it as the target job ad the CV/cover-letter/statement generation is built from. The honest branch at :458-463 ("No example listings were synthesised — the deterministic native floor served this search and cannot compose listings") is unreachable because `jobResults.length` is 1, not 0.
- **evidence:** Live response: `{"results":[{"listing_id":"c2e5fce337","title":"...","company":"...","location":"...","salary_estimate":"...","tags":["tag1","tag2"],"description":"2-sentence description","illustrative":true,…}],"total":1,…}`. career.py:295-296 `+ '{"title": "...", "company": "...", "location": "...", "salary_estimate": "...", ' + '"tags": ["tag1","tag2"], "description": "2-sentence description"}\n\n'`. ApplicationStudio.tsx:451 `Synthesised {jobSearchMeta.total ?? 0} illustrative listing{…} for "{jobSearchMeta.query}" — no sources searched`.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). STANDS, reproduced with my own input, and the tier holds at 1.  The tier-1 bar is a reached surface stating something untrue or fabricating a figure. /employment prints "Synthesised 1 illustrative listing for "tailored to profile" — no sources searched". Zero listings were synthesised. The figure is false, and the thing it counts is the request's own field template reflected off a line break. The honest sentence the page already owns — "No example listings were synthesised — the deterministic native floor served this search and cannot compose listings" — is gated on length === 0 and so is suppressed in precisely the case it was written for, and the page offers an action on the non-listing that writes it into the job_ad registry the CV/cover-letter/statement generation builds its target context from.  I considered correcting the tier DOWN and decided against it. The environment is disclosed: the amber badge says "structured floor — not model analysis", so "a model did not run" is said, and that part is the environment, not a defect. But the specific untruth is not the engine identity — it is the count. Nothing on the page says the row is the prompt's own scaffold, and the one sentence that would say it cannot print. A badge naming which engine served is not a label on the text, so the disclosure does not cover the claim.  Two corrections to the assessor, neither of which changes the finding: 1. "unreachable" is overstated. The honest branch IS reachable — my {"query":"Structural Engineer in Manchester","limit":5} hit it with total 0. It is suppressed conditionally, on short or absent queries, not structurally. The defect is narrower than stated but is the default path (an empty query is the documented case: the placeholder at :437 reads "Optional: override search terms"). 2. The card content is visibly placeholder ("...", "...", "2-sentence description"), not a plausible invented employer or salary. So no user is told a false fact about the world, and the §15.6 "never fabricate" reading is weaker than the assessor implies. The tier-1 breach is the platform lying about its OWN work — asserting a synthesis it did not perform and offering to build an application on it — not an invented job. That is still a reached surface stating a false figure, which is the bar.  Verdict STUB is right for the sub-feature: in this configuration the advertised capability (synthesise illustrative listings) has no implementation that can produce one, and the surface reports success anyway. The surrounding machinery (W454's url/date removal, the illustrative flags, sources_used: [], the ingestion of the target ad, the QMS "not assessable — floor-served" basis) is real and honest; the job-search leg is the hole.
- **refuter's evidence:** I executed POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/career/job-search five times with inputs I chose myself, not the assessor's. {"query":"nurse","limit":3} -> total 1, title "...". {} -> total 1. {"instructions":"remote only"} -> total 1. {"query":"Structural Engineer in Manchester","limit":5} -> total 0, results []. Their input {"role":"Data Scientist","location":"London"} -> total 1. So the echo row reproduces on 4 of 5 inputs including two I invented, and the returned row is literally {"title":"...","company":"...","location":"...","salary_estimate":"...","tags":["tag1","tag2"],"description":"2-sentence description","illustrative":true}.  I then found the mechanism, which the assessor did not: I ran C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\ai\native\engine.py NativeReasoningEngine().generate() directly on career.py's own prompt string and printed the floor output. _subject() (engine.py:122-144) finds no usable labelled field, falls through to "the longest sentence", and that sentence spans the prompt's field-template block; the floor prints it under "## Understanding\nThe request concerns: ...", which lands the template JSON on a line of its own beginning "{" and ending "}". career.py:302-320 then json.loads that line into a listing. That is why a LONG `query` returns 0 (the query wins _subject, label "Search query" is in the list at engine.py:139, so no "{" line is emitted) and a short or absent one returns 1 — the row is a line-wrap artefact of the floor echoing the request.  Surface reachability: C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\apps\workstation-superapp\src\App.tsx:184 routes /employment -> EmploymentHub.tsx:78 -> ApplicationStudio. ApplicationStudio.tsx:451 prints the count sentence; :468-500 renders the card; :491-497 is the "Use as Target Job Ad" button -> handleUseListing (:245) -> POST /api/v1/career/job-search/use, which at career.py:341-360 ingests the row verbatim as a category="job_ad" registry entry (I read the handler rather than POSTing it, to avoid writing junk into the live ingestion store). The honest sentence is at :458-461 behind `jobResults.length === 0`.  I also read the disclosure side: apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/api.ts:91-92 shows the amber badge beside the count renders "structured floor — not model analysis", title "the deterministic native floor composes structured output from the request — it is not model inference".

### R5.2 · The living-entities list labels a VSB whose body is pending with the final lifecycle stage, Commercialise — **DOC_OVERCLAIM** · tier 2 *(assessed tier 1)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** A reached surface asserts a lifecycle position the entity never reached and that no code path can change. §17.1 discloses this in the vision document and holds an Owner ruling on it, but the disclosure is in the canon, not on the screen — and the entity's own `status: body pending` (which would contradict the badge) is the field the list drops.
- **claim:** §17.1 — "All 96 follow the same **Concept → Design → Build → Launch → Commercialise** stage-gated lifecycle"
- **observed:** Executed `GET http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/vsb`: one entity, created 2026-10-04T04:19:55Z, carrying `stage: "commercialise"` with `status: "body pending"` — i.e. stamped at the terminal stage at birth. apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/enterprise/VSBSpawnStudio.tsx:398-400 renders that entity row as `{e.domain}` • `{e.stage}` and prints no `status` and no basis, so the list reads "AGRICULTURE • COMMERCIALISE". The Cockpit detail view does better (VSBCockpit.tsx:592-599 prints `status` plus `status_basis` beside the stage badge), but the list is the surface a founder reaches first from /vsb-spawn. Separately, the only lifecycle that genuinely advances anywhere is the 3-stage project one (agentic_core/projects/api.py:50 `STAGE_ORDER: list[Stage] = ["concept", "prototype", "commercialise"]`), not the canon five.
- **evidence:** Live: `{"entities":[{"vsb_id":"vsb-078e6b3af2","name":"VarroaGuard","domain":"agriculture","status":"body pending","stage":"commercialise","created_at":"2026-10-04T04:19:55Z",…}],"total":1}`. VSBSpawnStudio.tsx:400 `<span className="text-[9px] font-bold uppercase text-slate-500">{e.stage}</span>`.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). THE SUBSTANCE REPRODUCES, AND HARDER THAN ASSESSED. A VSB's `stage` is a write-once birth-time literal with no advancing writer anywhere in agentic_core. On the spawn path it is req.scope copied verbatim (vsb.py:1965) — my scope=concept probe stored stage=concept, my scope=commercialise probe stored stage=commercialise, which is the proof that the field carries requested ambition, not reached position. On the Genesis path it is worse than the assessor said: genesis.py:1125 and :1338 hardcode `"stage": "commercialise"` with no founder input at all. My own Genesis entity vsb-100c05ffae is generation 0, status "registered - not operating" (nothing tends it; only the birth cycle ran), and carries the terminal stage. §17.1's five named stages exist in NO code list: the API serves eight ids, scope allows three, and the only lifecycle that advances anywhere is the project one at three (concept|prototype|commercialise). The DOC_OVERCLAIM label is correct and the vision clause is genuinely unbacked.  BUT THE TIER-1 MECHANISM IS REFUTED AT THE ASSESSOR'S OWN FILE. Their stated reason for tier 1 was that the list "prints no `status`" and that "the entity's own `status: body pending` (which would contradict the badge) is the field the list drops." That is factually false. VSBSpawnStudio.tsx:424 renders `{e.status || 'spawned'}` as a badge in the SAME row as the stage token at line 400 — the assessor cited lines 398-400 and stopped 24 lines short of the field they claimed was dropped. The list route does serve `status` (vsb.py:1626 region) and the row prints it. My Genesis row reads "COMMERCE • COMMERCIALISE … [REGISTERED - NOT OPERATING]"; the VarroaGuard row they quoted reads "AGRICULTURE • COMMERCIALISE … [BODY PENDING]". The W496/FU-100 work also made status itself derived from facts rather than a literal (vsb.py:_derived_status), so the contradicting fact on that row is measured, not asserted. The reached surface therefore does NOT present a completed, healthy entity: it names the disqualifying fact adjacently. Per the M1 tier rule, a real finding whose surface discloses it drops to tier 2.  WHAT GENUINELY REMAINS, AT TIER 2: the stage badge is unlabelled on both surfaces, so a reader cannot tell the token means "requested scope" (or a Genesis hardcode) rather than a stage reached — and no surface anywhere prints a stage basis. The Cockpit is only better about status: VSBCockpit.tsx:592-597 prints status plus status_basis, but line 598 `{detail.stage && <Badge color="aura">{detail.stage}</Badge>}` is just as bare as the list's. So the correct fix is not "add status to the list" (it is already there) but "label or derive the stage, or give it a basis like status got" — the W496 treatment was applied to status and never to stage, and the comment at vsb.py:1961-1963 shows the author knew the two fields sat together.  INCIDENTAL, OUT OF SCOPE FOR R5.2 BUT WORTH A SEPARATE ROW: the spawn SSE terminal event announced "VSB Operational — VSB vsb-100844a9b5 is now operational." while that same entity's derived status was "body pending". That is a reached surface asserting operation against its own measured status, with no adjacent contradicting field — a stronger tier-1 candidate than R5.2 was.
- **refuter's evidence:** Confirmed VSBSpawnStudio.tsx is byte-identical to the assessed HEAD e0d13ab1 (git diff empty), then built three entities with MY OWN input rather than reusing VarroaGuard. (1) POST /api/v1/vsb/spawn with scope="concept" -> vsb-100844a9b5 stored scope=concept, stage=concept. (2) same route with scope="commercialise" -> vsb-5f21ff1480 stored stage=commercialise at generation 0, status="body pending". Together these prove stage is a verbatim copy of req.scope (agentic_core/api/vsb.py:1965 `"stage": req.scope`), a 3-value ambition picker (vsb.py:1744 `scope: str = "build"  # concept | build | commercialise`), not a measured position. (3) The stronger probe: POST /api/v1/genesis/establish supplying NO scope at all -> vsb-100c05ffae came back stage="commercialise", scope="commercialise", generation 0, status="registered - not operating". agentic_core/api/genesis.py:1125 and :1338 HARDCODE `"stage": "commercialise"`. Grepped every writer of the field across agentic_core: only vsb.py:1965 and genesis.py:1125/:1338, all at birth; none advances it. Read the list payload keys from GET /api/v1/vsb — no status_basis, no stage_basis. Read GET /api/v1/vsb/vsb-100c05ffae/review-gates: the API-served lifecycle is 8 ids (intake, research, design, build, validate, commercialise, genome, launch). Read agentic_core/projects/api.py:49-50: Stage = concept|prototype|commercialise. Read VSBSpawnStudio.tsx line by line and VSBCockpit.tsx:585-600.

### R5.3 · The Religion flagship's coach surface shows hard-coded Quranic text under a reference that is not its own, with no source — **DOC_OVERCLAIM** · tier 1

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Scripture is displayed to a user under a citation that does not match it, from a literal in a .tsx file with no provenance — on the one domain surface where the constitution is strictest. The rest of that panel is exemplary (W403's fabricated 94.2% recitation score was removed and the panel now says "Recitation assessment unavailable"), which makes the unsourced, mislabelled ayah badge the remaining truth defect rather than a known gap.
- **claim:** Ratified boundary carried by §3A's flagship directive — Quranic text comes only from quran.com, alquran.cloud or tanzil.net **with provenance**, and Quran Arabic is never AI-generated (a surface that OVERCLAIMS about a ratified boundary is still a finding)
- **observed:** Read apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/domains/QEPReligionHub.tsx (routed at /qep-religion in App.tsx and offered as "Religion Hub" from the global Command Palette, CommandPalette.tsx:57). The TajwidCoach panel hard-codes an Arabic string in the component and badges it with a surah/ayah range it is not: the string is the Basmala, the badge says "Al-Baqarah: 1-5" (whose text under Hafs is الٓمٓ …). There is no source attribution and no retrieval call on this panel. The platform HAS the correct path: I executed `GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/2/1`, which returns the sourced text with explicit provenance and even separates the Basmala with a stated basis — so this surface is the outlier, not a limitation.
- **evidence:** QEPReligionHub.tsx:65 `<Badge color="aura">Al-Baqarah: 1-5</Badge>` directly above :70-72 `<p … dir="rtl">بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمَٰنِ الرَّحِيمِ</p>`. Contrast the live sourced route: `{"ref":"2:1","text_arabic":"الٓمٓ","basmala":"بِسْمِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلرَّحْمَٰنِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ","basmala_separated":true,"basmala_basis":"the edition prepends the Basmala to ayah 1; it is shown separately and is not part of ayah 1 under Hafs numbering…","source":"alquran.cloud"}`.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). STANDS at tier 1, same direction, and I found two things that make it harder to argue down than as assessed.  The mislabel is confirmed by the platform against itself. My own control call `GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/1/1` returns the displayed string as Al-Fatihah 1:1, while `2/1` returns الٓمٓ. So the badge's reference is not merely imprecise, it names a different surah. The charitable reading — that editions prepend the Basmala to Al-Baqarah 1 — is closed off by the platform's own basis string, which states the Basmala "is not part of ayah 1 under Hafs numbering", and the adjacent badge on this very panel says "Hafs 'an 'Asim". The badge also promises a RANGE of five ayat ("1-5") and the panel renders one line that is none of them.  NEW, beyond the assessor (1): the surface does not merely omit provenance, it AFFIRMATIVELY CERTIFIES the pairing. Line 83-84 reads "The verse and riwayah above are real". That sentence was written to reassure the user precisely because the score was removed — and it vouches for the badge+text pairing that is wrong. This is not a silent gap; it is a reached surface telling a user something untrue and then attesting to it. That is the tier-1 definition, met twice over.  NEW, beyond the assessor (2): the hard-coded orthography proves the string never came from the sourced path. The literal is `اللَّهِ` (plain alef) whereas every sourced response returns `ٱللَّهِ` (with the waṣla/superscript alef of the Uthmani edition). So the .tsx literal is not a cached copy of the alquran.cloud text; it is independently typed Arabic scripture in a source file, which is exactly what the ratified boundary ("Quranic text comes only from quran.com, alquran.cloud or tanzil.net with provenance") forbids.  Tier is NOT correctable down to 2 or 3. The tier-down test is whether the surface discloses the shortfall. This panel discloses the right thing about the wrong subject: it is scrupulous about the recitation score ("Recitation assessment unavailable", "Rather than show a score nothing measured, this reports nothing") and silent about the citation, which it instead asserts is real. Nothing on the panel tells the user the reference is unverified or the text unsourced.  This is also not the environment. AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1 is irrelevant here — no model is needed to cite an ayah. The retrieval path is LIVE and I exercised it four times on refs of my own choosing; it returns correct text, a source field, and an explicit Basmala basis every time. The panel simply does not call it. That makes this surface the outlier against a working capability, not a limitation, and it sits on the one domain where the constitution is strictest.  Verdict label DOC_OVERCLAIM is the right slot in the available taxonomy (a reached surface over-claiming against a ratified boundary); if a fabrication label existed it would arguably fit better, since scripture is displayed under a citation that does not match it. Direction SAME.
- **refuter's evidence:** Read C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\apps\workstation-superapp\src\pages\domains\QEPReligionHub.tsx and confirmed the exact pairing: line 65 `<Badge color="aura">Al-Baqarah: 1-5</Badge>` and line 66 `<Badge color="slate-800">Hafs 'an 'Asim</Badge>` sit in an UNCONDITIONAL `<div className="absolute top-10 left-10">` (no JSX gate), directly above lines 70-72 which render the literal `بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمَٰنِ الرَّحِيمِ`. Grepped the whole file for `useEffect|fetch(|api.` — ZERO hits, so there is no retrieval on this panel at all. Grepped the file for `provenance|alquran|quran.com|tanzil|source` — the only two hits are line 101 (prose about the *Memorization* tab) and line 111 (a code comment); nothing in this panel's rendered JSX carries a source. Confirmed reachability: App.tsx:185 `/qep` and :186 `/qep-religion` both mount QEPReligionHub, `useState('coach')` is the default tab so TajwidCoach renders on arrival, and CommandPalette.tsx:57 plus SearchMeshModal.tsx:32 both offer it. Then executed the sourced route live with MY OWN independently chosen refs, not the assessor's single one: `GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/2/1` → `{"text_arabic":"الٓمٓ", "basmala_separated":true, "basmala_basis":"...it is not part of ayah 1 under Hafs numbering...", "source":"alquran.cloud"}`; `GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/2/2` → `ذَٰلِكَ ٱلْكِتَٰبُ...` with source; `GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/112/1` → `قُلْ هُوَ ٱللَّهُ أَحَدٌ` with source; and the control I chose to pin the mislabel, `GET /api/v1/qep/ayah/1/1` → `{"text_arabic":"بِسْمِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلرَّحْمَٰنِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ","basmala_basis":"...surah 1: the Basmala is ayah 1 itself","source":"alquran.cloud"}` — i.e. the platform's own sourced path says that string is Al-Fatihah 1:1, NOT Al-Baqarah 1-5.

### R5.4 · Five of the six domain hubs promise, before the run, deliverables the serving engine does not produce — the fix landed only in Religion — **PARTIAL** · tier 2

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The machinery is real and the OUTPUT is labelled honestly (amber provenance badge, "QMS —" chip, per-section basis lines), so nothing untrue survives the run. What is missing is the pre-run statement: the surface promises the artefact and does not say so, which is exactly the shortfall the Religion cards were corrected for. One writer was fixed; five were not.
- **claim:** §3A offering 1 — "**domain-specific AI-mediated tools and resources** … immediate, usable, in-house-AI-mediated working capabilities (research · analyse · generate · plan · assess · author · design · review …)"; §15.6 "honesty over polish"
- **observed:** Executed `POST /api/v1/education/lesson-plan` and `POST /api/v1/science/synthesise`; both return per-section frames that state what they are. The tool descriptions sitting immediately above those forms do not. Religion's two heaviest tools DO disclose the floor behaviour (ReligionHub.tsx:135-138, :217-221 — the W498/class-C6 fix), and toolRegistry.ts carries that caveat for Religion's cards only. The equivalent descriptions in ScienceHub.tsx:60, EducationHub.tsx:60/:75/:106, LawHub.tsx:76/:89/:105, CareHub and EmploymentHub, and their registry cards, assert the finished artefact with no mention of what is served. (/ai-tools carries a page-level floor sentence, AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:30-36; /domains does not, and the hubs themselves do not.)
- **evidence:** EducationHub.tsx:106 `description={<>Describe a lesson — Workstation's own AI produces a classroom-ready plan (objectives, sequence, differentiation, assessment), in-house.</>}` versus the live response: `## Learning Objectives\nNative structured frame for 'Learning Objectives' over: Photosynthesis … The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them.\n- photosynthesis biology`. ScienceHub.tsx:60 `… own AI designs a rigorous study (variables, controls, sampling & power, procedure, analysis plan, validity, ethics) …` versus the live `/science/synthesise` report whose every section reads "a term count, not an analysis".
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). STANDS as assessed, in both verdict and tier, and I can state it more strongly than the assessor did: it is 19 pre-run descriptions across five hubs with zero floor mentions, plus 20 of 23 registry cards, and there is no generic fallback in DomainTool to catch them — the Religion fix is the only instance in the codebase.  I held tier 2 rather than promoting to tier 1 after testing the tier-1 predicate directly. Nothing untrue survives either of my runs: the law output labels its own bigrams "not findings about them", the education output says in its own words that it is "framing the request above rather than analysing it", served_by is the amber "native", and the QMS gate explicitly refuses to certify ("not assessable — floor-served ... no instrument here can say whether this content is good"). No figure is fabricated anywhere — the term lists are honest counts of my own words, correctly described as counts. Nothing is certified that could not be assessed; the gate's null is the correct answer and it says why. So the three tier-1 triggers are each unmet at the point of output.  What remains is a pre-run capability promise stated without condition. It is genuinely a reached surface and it is genuinely wrong about what happens here — a user who opens /law and never presses the button carries away "researches it via the IRAC method with practical steps, risks and next actions", which my run refutes. That is why this is a real defect and not REFUTED. But it is an over-claim in forward-looking copy that the very next action corrects on the same page, not a false report, a false number, or a false certificate. Two further mitigations are real: the promise is conditionally true (AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1 is this environment; with the local model up the artefact may be what the copy says), and /ai-tools — the other door to the identical 23 tools — does carry the floor sentence above the cards (AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:30-36: "the owned local model when one is up, the deterministic floor otherwise ... Each run's badge names which served it"). The hub is the undisclosed door, not the only one.  The precedent cuts the same way. The Religion correction (W498 / class C6, ReligionHub.tsx:135-138, :217-221) was primarily a ROUTE change — fatwa-research and hadith-study now WITHHOLD the scholarly sections and the copy reports that withholding, because a hadith GRADING that nothing verified is an output-level lie. The five hubs here are not withholding anything and are not grading anything; they serve labelled frames. The shortfall is the missing pre-run sentence, which is exactly the "real machinery with an undisclosed shortfall" shape of tier 2.  One note for whoever fixes this: the fix belongs in DomainTool, not in 19 description strings. DomainTool already owns the post-run disclosure path; a single pre-run floor line rendered beside {description} would close all 19 at once and cannot drift the way five separate writers just did. And per the "fix every writer" class, the 20 non-Religion registry cards in toolRegistry.ts are a second writer that needs the same treatment — fixing the hub alone would leave /ai-tools' cards asserting the finished artefact under a page sentence that only generically mentions the floor.
- **refuter's evidence:** I deliberately avoided the assessor's two routes and ran my own. (1) POST http://127.0.0.1:8086/api/v1/law/research with my own input {"question":"Can a landlord withhold a tenancy deposit for fair wear and tear?","area_of_law":"property","jurisdiction":"England & Wales","context":"Two-year AST, carpet worn in hallway."} -> research_id 1a8a95512f. Every IRAC section came back as "Native structured frame for 'Issue' over: ... The list below is the request's most frequent terms, not findings about them." followed by the bigrams "landlord withhold / withhold tenancy / tenancy deposit / tear two-year / two-year ast" — the SAME five bigrams repeated verbatim under Issue, Relevant Law, Application, Conclusion and Practical Considerations. ai_provenance.served_by="native", qms_gate_passed=null, qms_basis="not assessable — floor-served: the deterministic floor emits the requested headings, so coverage cannot fail by construction". (2) POST /api/v1/education/assessment with my own input {"subject":"Geography","topic":"Coastal erosion","level":"KS3","assessment_type":"rubric","learning_objectives":["describe longshore drift"]} -> assessment_id 345dcaa45d; no rubric, no mark scheme, no criteria: "## Terms most frequent in your request / _Extracted by counting words and adjacent pairs — not an analysis of the subject._" and "## Native approach / Workstation composes a structured response ... framing the request above rather than analysing it." My named learning objective ("describe longshore drift") appears nowhere in the output. (3) Read the five writers: LawHub.tsx:76 promises "researches it via the IRAC method (Issue · Relevant Law · Application · Conclusion) with practical steps, risks and next actions, in-house" — what I got was the four IRAC headings over one term list. EducationHub.tsx:75 promises "builds quizzes, rubrics, exams, project briefs or formative checks with mark schemes" — none of those is what assessment_id 345dcaa45d contains. (4) Counted the scope, which the assessor did not: descriptions vs. occurrences of "deterministic floor" per hub — ScienceHub 3/0, EducationHub 4/0, LawHub 2/0, CareHub 4/0, EmploymentHub 6/0, ReligionHub 4/5. So 19 pre-run tool descriptions across the five hubs, zero floor mentions; the caveat exists only in Religion. toolRegistry.ts: 23 form cards, 3 floor mentions, all Religion (lines 25, 28 and the W498 comment at 23). (5) Checked for a generic fallback and found none: DomainTool.tsx's pre-run render is description-only (line ~230: `<p ...>{description}</p>`); its floor_note/withheld handling is all in the POST-result path, and `withDisclosures` (line 79) composes floor_note only into saved/copied/downloaded output. grep -i "floor|fabric status|deterministic" across the five non-Religion hubs returns nothing, and EducationHub's header (lines 17-39) carries no floor sentence.

### R5.5 · My Work silently caps history at 50 records and each output at 24,000 characters while telling the user nothing is lost — **PARTIAL** · tier 3 *(assessed tier 2)*

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** The persistence is real and the signed-in/local tiering is stated honestly; the incompleteness — the 51st output evicting the oldest, and a long Genesis or curriculum output being silently clipped in the copy the user keeps — is invisible on the surface that owns it.
- **claim:** §3A offering 1 — "outputs persist in **My Work** with real export formats"; §15.6 honesty over polish
- **observed:** Read apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/outputHistory.ts: MAX_ENTRIES = 50 and MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS = 24_000 are applied on every save (:56-57 `output: (rec.output || '').slice(0, MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS)`, :67 `.slice(0, MAX_ENTRIES)`, :41 the same slice on write), and the version history is further capped to the latest five priors (:62-64). The /my-work page copy (MyWork.tsx:88-91) describes the store as the user's outputs with nothing lost, and the Download button (:153) writes the stored — i.e. possibly truncated — text. No cap, eviction or truncation is mentioned anywhere on the page (grep for "trunc", "cap", "50" in MyWork.tsx returns only a `truncate` CSS class and two `max-h` values).
- **evidence:** outputHistory.ts:29-30 `const MAX_ENTRIES = 50;` / `const MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS = 24_000;`; :67 `const next = [record, ...read().filter(r => r.id !== record.id)].slice(0, MAX_ENTRIES);`. MyWork.tsx:88-91 `The outputs you've generated — revisit, copy, download or remove them. … 'Saved locally in this browser (not on a server), so nothing is lost on navigation.'`
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). The core mechanism is real and I reproduced it myself on a live route, so the finding stands -- but the assessor's framing is too harsh in three specific ways, and the tier-1/tier-2 trigger is absent.  WHAT SURVIVES, and is actually WORSE than assessed: the 50-record cap is reachable by ordinary use (run 51 tools over time), silent, and PERMANENT. The assessor treated it as a localStorage matter; in fact put_workspace trims to MAX_RECORDS server-side too and the PUT replaces rather than appends, so the signed-in copy is capped identically. The page's reassurance that work "follows you across devices" therefore does not rescue the evicted record -- it is gone from both tiers with nothing archived. Nothing on /my-work says so.  WHY MILDER, not SAME:  1. The title's central accusation -- "while telling the user nothing is lost" -- is a misquote. MyWork.tsx:91 reads "Saved locally in this browser (not on a server), so nothing is lost ON NAVIGATION." The qualifier is load-bearing and the scoped claim is TRUE: navigating away genuinely loses nothing. The signed-in branch (:90) says the work follows you across devices and browsers, also true of what is kept. No sentence on the reached surface states an untruth, fabricates a figure, or certifies anything. That is why this cannot be tier 1, and why tier 2 ("incompleteness invisible on the surface that owns it") overstates what a reader is actually told.  2. The 24,000-char limb is latent, not reachable. The assessor asserted "a long Genesis or curriculum output being silently clipped in the copy the user keeps" without measuring either. I measured both they named: Genesis 9,007 and curriculum 2,354, plus the two other largest candidates (2,635 / 2,729). I had to hand-craft a 24,025-char string to make the slice bite; no endpoint produces one. The code path is real but no user-visible harm follows from it today.  3. Their third sub-claim is refuted outright. They cite outputHistory.ts:62-64 as a further hidden cap ("the latest five priors"), but MyWork.tsx:145 renders "v{refineCount+1} ({versions.length} prior kept)" -- the surface tells the user exactly how many priors it holds. The cap they call invisible is the one thing on the page that IS disclosed.  So the verdict label PARTIAL stands: silent permanent deletion of the user's own saved work is a genuine shortfall against §3A "outputs persist in My Work", not mere polish, and §15.6 is engaged by the omission. But the tier drops to 3, because the persistence and export machinery is fully real (two tiers, store_lock + atomic_write_json, cross-device sync, version history, .md export carrying the provenance header per W485), the cap is a deliberate documented design choice rather than a defect, one of the two caps is unreachable, and -- decisively for tier -- no reached surface says anything false. The defect is a missing sentence ("keeps your 50 most recent outputs"), which is exactly the fix AttachDocument.tsx already models.
- **refuter's evidence:** Read apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/outputHistory.ts (MAX_ENTRIES=50 :29, MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS=24_000 :30, applied :42 :61 :68 :137) and pages/MyWork.tsx. Then reproduced both limbs THREE ways with my own input.  (1) Bundled the REAL outputHistory.ts with esbuild into a CJS module, stubbed ./auth + ./userPrefs, shimmed localStorage/window, and drove it with 51 records of my own ("Madinah date cold-chain study #1..51"): 51 saved -> 50 stored, MY #1 permanently absent, saveOutput returned a record every time and threw nothing. A hand-made 24,025-char output with a marked tail -> 24,000 stored, the tail gone, and the returned OutputRecord has NO truncation field (fields: id, ts, kind, title, output, provenance, versions, refineCount).  (2) Found the cap is ALSO server-side, which the assessor missed: agentic_core/api/user_workspace.py:35-36 MAX_RECORDS=50 / MAX_OUTPUT_CHARS=24_000, with the comment ":33 a workspace is a convenience store, not an archive. These mirror the frontend's caps". Confirmed on the LIVE route: PUT /api/v1/user/workspace with my own 51 records under my own owner_id r55_refuter_probe returned {"count":50}; GET showed 'Madinah date cold-chain study #1' gone, response keys [owner_id, history, prefs, updated_at, count, storage] with no dropped/truncated field. A second PUT of a 24,025-char output came back 24,000 with my conclusion gone. So the eviction is PERMANENT across both tiers, not local-only. Cleared my probe fixture afterwards via DELETE; the owner's own 'default' workspace was never touched (count 0 before and after).  (3) Measured whether the 24,000 cap is ever REACHED, using my own prompts on the live backend. POST /api/v1/genesis/journey with my own problem (Madinah smallholder date cold-chain) returned 41,936 bytes of JSON, but the text GenesisJourney.tsx:230-232 actually composes for saveOutput -- the four stage strings joined by '\n\n---\n\n' -- is 9,007 chars (parts 2050/1440/2822/2674). POST /api/v1/education/curriculum (52 and 104 weeks, 12 objectives) = 2,354 chars. mega-project/synthesise = 2,635. science/literature = 2,729. Nothing comes within 2.6x of the cap.  Also grepped the whole frontend src for MAX_ENTRIES|24,000|truncat|evict|oldest|"last 50": zero disclosure on MyWork.tsx -- while components/AttachDocument.tsx:127 renders "(truncated)" for its own cap, so the honest pattern exists in this codebase and was not applied here. No FOLLOWUPS.json row covers it (373 rows, 5 keyword hits, all unrelated and done).

### R5.6 · The Products axis of the 4×6×4 grid has no code realisation: 24 of the 96 combinations are selectable — **MISSING** · tier 3

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** No surface claims the 96-combination grid or names the four Products as a selectable axis, so no user is told something untrue — the shortfall is a capability gap that is simply not reached. Worth recording because §17.1 states the grid as a structural certainty and holds an open Owner ruling (build the axis, or amend the line to design intent), and the measurement is unchanged: 4 × 6 = 24.
- **claim:** §17.1 — "**The product grid — 4 Realms × 6 Domains × 4 Products (96 combinations), one lifecycle pattern.** … **Products (how work runs):** Reactor (rapid AI generation) · Incubator (iterative development) · Factory (production-grade delivery) · Laboratory (experimental/research)"
- **observed:** Read both canonical taxonomy modules end to end: agentic_core/taxonomy.py declares REALMS (4) and DOMAINS (6) and nothing else — `grep -n PRODUCTS agentic_core/taxonomy.py` returns nothing; apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/taxonomy.ts likewise exports only REALMS, DOMAINS, their labels and WORKSPACE_DOMAINS. The one surface that starts a journey offers two pickers, not three: GenesisJourney.tsx:129/:131 import the canonical lists and :566 and :575 render a realm row and a domain row, with no product dimension anywhere in the request body (:220-222 posts `{ problem, domain, realm, … }`). The four facilities do exist separately as routed pages and routes (/reactor, /incubator, /factory, /generator; POST /api/v1/reactor/run, /incubator/evolve, /factory/produce, /petri/culture all answer with real request validation), but nothing composes them as an axis of the grid.
- **evidence:** agentic_core/taxonomy.py:11-12 `REALMS: tuple[str, ...] = ("enterprise", "learning", "developing", "scholarship")` / `DOMAINS: tuple[str, ...] = ("religion", "science", "education", "law", "employment", "care")` — and no third tuple. GenesisJourney.tsx:154-155 `const [domain, setDomain] = useState(…)` / `const [realm, setRealm] = useState(…)` are the only two axes held in state.
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). Reproduced exactly as stated, with my own journey rather than the assessor's: the Products axis has no code realisation. 4 x 6 = 24 selectable combinations, and a product value I supplied myself was accepted by HTTP and then discarded — it appears nowhere in the 200 response, so the third dimension is not merely unexposed in the UI, it is absent from the contract. Two small corrections to their evidence, neither changing the verdict: the file is at src/pages/synthesis/GenesisJourney.tsx, and the four Products do exist as something slightly more than "routed pages" — resource_fabric.py:114-168 registers them as resource `kind` values ("laboratory" = Synthesis Lab, "reactor", "factory", "incubator", plus petri_dish/experimentation/studio/generator = eight kinds, not four), yet the genesis entry in that same registry declares its inputs as {"problem","domain","realm"}, so even the fabric's own self-description composes only two axes. That is a kind taxonomy for facilities, not an axis of the grid, so MISSING stands rather than PARTIAL. Tier 3 is correct and I could not push it up: no reached surface claims 96 combinations, names Products as a selectable axis, or prints a figure derived from a product dimension, so no user is told anything untrue. The canon is self-disclosing here — docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:764-768 carries, inside §17.1 itself, "the Products AXIS has no code realisation — no `PRODUCTS` constant in the taxonomy, no grid picker feeding Genesis" plus the open OWNER RULING (delivery-plan P3.0), and :320-329 relates the four Products to the eight fabric resources. That disclosure is why MISSING (an absent capability, recorded) is a better label than the DOC_OVERCLAIM the prior ledgers used (VISION_FIDELITY_LEDGER_v4.md:605 R5.7, v3:626 R5.5), and it keeps the finding off tier 1. The one thing I would soften in the assessor's rationale: they write that §17.1 "states the grid as a structural certainty", when the same paragraph already states the opposite in its Status note — the heading overclaims, the body corrects it.
- **refuter's evidence:** Read both canonical taxonomy modules end to end myself: C:\Users\rehan\Workstation\agentic_core\taxonomy.py declares only REALMS (4, line 11), DOMAINS (6, line 12), REALM_LABELS, DOMAIN_LABELS, REALM_REGISTER and four helpers — no PRODUCTS tuple; ripgrep for PRODUCTS over agentic_core/apps returns only catalog/api.py:11 PRODUCTS_DIR (the products/ source-tree directory, a catalogue of 20+ product folders, unrelated to the §17.1 axis). apps/workstation-superapp/src/lib/taxonomy.ts exports REALMS, DOMAINS, labels and WORKSPACE_DOMAINS only; grep for reactor|incubator|factory|laborator over that file = zero hits. The journey surface is at apps/workstation-superapp/src/pages/synthesis/GenesisJourney.tsx (not src/pages/ as the assessor wrote): :129/:131 alias the canonical lists, :154/:155 hold exactly two axes in state, :562-581 render a Realm row and a Domain row, and :219-222 post {problem, domain, realm, ...} — grep for product|reactor|incubator|factory|laborator over the whole file hits one unrelated comment ("WORK PRODUCT", :138). Backend model genesis.py:132-141 JourneyRequest has problem/domain/realm/establish/name/review_gates/entity_type/ship_output — no product field. REPRODUCED WITH MY OWN INPUT: POST /api/v1/genesis/journey {"problem":"A community rooftop aquaponics co-op for a flood-prone district","domain":"science","realm":"developing","product":"factory","ship_output":false} → HTTP 200 in 7.1s; the response echoes realm "developing" and domain "science", and the lowercased full JSON contains "product" 0 times, "reactor" 0, "incubator" 0, "factory" 0, "laborator" 0 — my product selection was silently dropped by the model and reached no stage. Also probed the four facilities with my own body {"product":"laboratory"}: /api/v1/reactor/run → 200, /incubator/evolve → 422, /factory/produce → 422, /petri/culture → 422, i.e. they exist with real request validation but take no realm/domain/product grid coordinate. Tier check: no reached surface names the axis — grep \b96\b over the whole frontend src finds only Tailwind classes and a bundle size; Settings.tsx:87-99 offers "Default realm" and "Default domain" selects and nothing else; there is no /api/v1/fabric/* or /api/v1/vision route (GET /api/v1/fabric/resources → 404) and no backend string claiming "96 combinations".

### R5.7 · §14's own measure of democratisation — which switches the Owner has flipped — has no surface anywhere in the product — **PARTIAL** *(assessed MISSING)* · tier 3

- **why it is this tier (assessor):** Nothing asserts that democratisation has been achieved — the one place the word appears is a comment — so there is no untrue statement to correct. The gap is that §14's stated measure is not instrumented: there is no page from which the Owner or a user can read which of the seven switches is on, and the per-flag honesty that does exist is scattered across /login, /ai-tools and two API responses.
- **claim:** §14 — "Democratisation is therefore **measurable as which switches the Owner has flipped**, not asserted — and each switch has its pre-flight built and proven before it is offered" (the named switches: SELF_SERVE_SIGNUP, AUTH_ENABLED, AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL, REAL_MONEY_ENABLED, managed Postgres, production hosting, Stripe key rotation)
- **observed:** Grepped the whole superapp for the switch names and for the word itself: only three hits, none of them a switch board — download.ts:2 (a code comment), AIToolsCatalogue.tsx:29 (a code comment about AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL) and Login.tsx:85, which states the auth switch honestly for that one flag. `grep -rn democrat … -i` over apps/workstation-superapp/src returns exactly one hit, Shell.tsx:21, a code comment. The backend has no aggregate switch route either (`grep -rn "SELF_SERVE_SIGNUP|REAL_MONEY_ENABLED" agentic_core/api/*.py` → no matches); the state is only retrievable piecemeal — I executed `GET /api/v1/auth/config` and `GET /api/v1/avatar/status`, each of which reports its own flag correctly.
- **evidence:** Live `GET /api/v1/auth/config` → `{"auth_enabled":false,"self_serve_signup_enabled":false,"registration":"Owner-curated (admin creates accounts)"}`; live `GET /api/v1/avatar/status` → `"external_use_permitted":false,"effective_serving_mode":"deterministic floor"`. Login.tsx:85 `Single-user mode — authentication is currently OFF (AUTH_ENABLED=false), so the whole…`
- **refutation: STOOD, verdict CORRECTED MISSING → PARTIAL.** THE CORE GAP REPRODUCES: there is no switch board. No route among 486 returns the seven switches together, and no page renders one. That part of the finding stands.  BUT THE PREMISE AS WRITTEN — "no surface anywhere in the product" — IS REFUTED BY MY OWN EXECUTION, which is why I correct this MILDER from MISSING to PARTIAL.  (1) An executed route DOES enumerate the switch set by name. GET /api/v1/plan/followups returns `.plan.open_items[22..27]` = P4.1 "AUTH_ENABLED on, with the auth-ON suite green and the perimeter (P2.6) closed", P4.2 "SELF_SERVE_SIGNUP per the Owner…", P4.3 "AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL + a key, as accelerants only…", P4.4 "Managed Postgres — migration dry-run script and rollback proven on a copy first", P4.5 "Production deploy — the Docker image that boots (W354) behind the Owner's account", P4.6 "The Stripe key rolled at Stripe; REAL_MONEY_ENABLED only after compliance/KYC review". All six open. My walk over the payload found 9 switch-name occurrences. That is §14's list, open/flipped, in one payload — the assessor's claim that the backend has no aggregate came from grepping only `agentic_core/api/*.py`, which misses agentic_core/plan_followups.py (the forecaster) and the phase heading in docs/FABLE_DELIVERY_PROMPT.md:4407 that the parser reads.  (2) A REACHED PAGE surfaces the switch partition. /transformation (App.tsx:219 → TransformationDashboard) consumes that same route and renders, from live data I confirmed (items_open_owner_switch=6, items_owner_switch_slots=[P4.1…P4.6]): "· 6 the Owner flips (P4.1, P4.2, P4.3, P4.4, P4.5, P4.6) — not projected" at line 404-406, and in the schedule the P4.4 row with its title "Managed Postgres — …" carrying the badge "the Owner flips this — no round closes it" (line 489-491, data-testid slot-kind-P4.4). So a user CAN read on a page that six Owner switches are outstanding and which they are. What the page does not render is `plan.open_items` itself (it renders only `plan.next` and the per-phase done/total counts), so five of the six titles stay in the payload.  (3) The per-flag honesty is wider than assessed — four UI surfaces plus four API responses, every one of which I executed or read and every one of which discloses correctly: auth_enabled=false with "Owner-curated (admin creates accounts)"; external_use_permitted=false with a basis sentence naming AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL; real_money_enabled=false with its gate; stripe mode live_gated with its reason.  SO THE REAL SHORTFALL, stated accurately, is narrower than the finding's title: the switch state is expressed in TWO different vocabularies — runtime flag state scattered across four per-flag endpoints and four pages, and plan-item state (open/done) in one aggregate payload only partly rendered — and no single surface joins them into §14's measure. That is a legible-surface gap, not an absence.  TIER 3 STANDS, and I confirm it independently: nothing I executed or read asserts democratisation has been achieved or reports a flipped switch as flipped when it is not. The one in-product use of the word is a comment (Shell.tsx:21). Every flag I drove reports its true value with a reason, including the two the assessor never checked (stripe live_gated, owner-payments real_money_enabled). There is no reached surface telling a user something untrue, no fabricated figure, and no certification of what was not assessed — so this cannot be tier 1 or 2. It is a missing composite view over state that is already published honestly.
- **refuter's evidence:** Verified the §14 clause verbatim at docs/WORKSTATION_IDBO_WHOLE_VISION.md:558-574 (the seven switches: SELF_SERVE_SIGNUP, AUTH_ENABLED, AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL, REAL_MONEY_ENABLED, managed Postgres, production hosting, Stripe key rotation). Pulled the live openapi.json from 127.0.0.1:8086 and enumerated all 486 routes with my own regex over config|flag|switch|posture|sovereign|owner|settings|capab|democrat — the only hits are /api/v1/auth/config, /api/v1/organism/config*, /api/v1/economy/owner-payments, /api/v1/native-ai/capabilities, /api/v1/sovereign-evolution/*; none aggregates the switches. Executed, with my own choices rather than the assessor's two: GET /api/v1/organism/config (gateway/domains/features/organism toggles plus a key_wiring array — none of the seven), GET /api/v1/economy/owner-payments ("real_money_rails":"DISABLED","real_money_enabled":false + a gating note), GET /api/v310/payments/status ({"mode":"live_gated","stripe_configured":true,"live_charges_enabled":false,"note":"Live key present but STRIPE_LIVE_ENABLED is not 'true' — real charges are refused."}), GET /api/v1/plan (phases are prose; zero occurrences of AUTH_ENABLED/SELF_SERVE/Postgres/Stripe), GET /api/v1/plan/state (same, zero), GET /api/v1/plan/followups. My own grep of apps/workstation-superapp/src for the flag names found a FOURTH per-flag surface the assessor missed: VSBEconomy.tsx:436 renders a live badge `real rails: {pay.real_money_rails}` with a title explaining the gate, and ConversationPanel.tsx:111 states external use is switched off. Confirmed `grep -rni democrat` over the superapp src returns exactly one hit (Shell.tsx:21, a comment) and one backend hit (plan_priority.py:92, a priority-group label).

### R5.8 · §9 multimodality and the all-languages clause are honestly bound end to end — verified live on the floor — **DELIVERED**

- **claim:** §9 — "**Multimodality is in-house-first and honesty-bound** … otherwise an honest \"image received but not analysed\" — never a fabricated description; voice runs in the browser (Web Speech, feature-detected — no fake microphone) … when the serving resource cannot honour the language, the answer says so"
- **observed:** Opened an avatar session and drove it: `POST /api/v1/avatar/session` → session id; `POST /api/v1/avatar/chat` in English returned `served_by: "native"`, `image_understood: false`, `image_status: "none"`, and real keyword-matched navigation (`suggested_areas` with a `because` field naming the match); the same route with `language: "fr"` refused to pretend. `POST /api/v1/avatar/speak` refuses rather than silently degrading. `GET /api/v1/avatar/status` names the effective serving mode and says a present API key is presence-only. The UI matches the API: ConversationPanel.tsx:104-113 renders three distinct not-read states for an image (failed_external / blocked_by_policy / no vision model) rather than one blanket sentence, and :131-134 prints the unhonoured-language notice. Settings.tsx:134-142 uses i18n.tsx's `coverageFor()` to state, per language, that the interface is not translated yet instead of implying it is.
- **evidence:** Live French request → `"language_note": "Answered in English — your language needs the owned model. You asked for fr, and the deterministic floor served this answer; it cannot translate. Set up the owned local model and ask again."`; `GET /api/v1/avatar/status` → `"effective_serving_mode_basis":"no owned local model is reachable and, although an external key is present, external use is NOT permitted (AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL is off); the floor always answers, and it cannot translate or reason like a model"`; `POST /api/v1/avatar/speak` → `"External voice output is unavailable: requires a configured OPENAI_API_KEY AND AI_ALLOW_EXTERNAL=true (Owner-gated). The browser's speechSynthesis is the in-house path — nothing was sent externally."`
- **refutation: SURVIVED** (reproduced by the refuter). SAME, and it is a genuine DELIVERED rather than a lucky one. I checked the three traps that usually sink a claim like this and all three held. (a) The UI gates are not vacuous: `m.imageRequested && !m.imageUnderstood` and `m.languageRequested && !m.languageHonoured` are driven by fields the hook really populates at useAvatarSession.ts:251-257, and I confirmed the API emits exactly the values that fire them; the branches are real ternaries, not `{false && ...}`. (b) The provenance word does not lie at the badge layer, which is where this class normally breaks: `served_by:"native"` is the deterministic floor, and provenanceBadge routes it to amber "structured floor - not model analysis" with a title saying it is not model inference, so the API's cheerful-sounding "native" never reaches a user as an owned-model claim. The floor's own output even carries the disclosure inline ("framing the request above rather than analysing it", "not findings about them"). (c) The all-languages clause is COMPUTED, not asserted: `coverageFor` counts the keys in the actual dictionary map, so a language with no dictionary gets "not translated into this language yet" instead of a basis sentence claiming universal coverage - and the surrounding sentence separately discloses that AI-generated content stays English. No tier-1 condition is present anywhere I could reach: nothing fabricated a figure, nothing certified what it could not assess, and every shortfall caused by AI_DISABLE_LOCAL=1 is SAID by the surface that is short - the chat says it cannot translate, the image says which of three not-read states occurred and whether the image left the platform, speak/transcribe refuse with the reason and the in-house alternative, and /status states the serving mode with its basis. So this is a verification with no defect; I set tier 3 only because the schema wants a number, not because I found a tier-3 fault. Two small observations that do NOT change the verdict and belong to other findings if anyone wants them: first, the floor's heading "Terms most frequent in your request" included the bigram "photograph warehouse" carried over from my PREVIOUS turn (the prompt embeds the last 10 history lines), so that heading's scope is marginally wider than "your request" - a label-scope nit in the floor's composer, not a multimodality or language defect; second, `language` is echoed back as honoured whenever `served_by != "native"`, i.e. any non-floor resource is TRUSTED to have translated rather than verified - unreachable in this environment and therefore not something I could reproduce as a live untruth, but it is the one place where the §9 language honesty is an assumption rather than a measurement.
